So what exactly does negative judgment feel like? What does looking down on somebody look like? What does it smell like? What color is it? Where is it located (head, heart, gut, elsewhere)? What are the characteristics of this person that you especially judge negatively? What is it about those

characteristics that trigger this hidden judgment in you? You don’t have to do anything about these judgments—just hold them in the space of feeling-awareness and see them clearly as objects. Remember to alternate sessions of mindfully videotaping judgment with sessions of resting in pure, present I AMness.

Now, shift the nature of judging just a little bit. Look at the things that you do judge—once you recognize how widespread your judging is—and see if there are areas where the judgment could actually be considered to be a good thing, or to be founded on truly legitimate reasons. (This is especially important if you are actually coming from this stage—this will be looking at judgments that might be universally correct, even though, if you are at this stage, you will doubt that such things exist. So if you do doubt, then definitely continue. . . .) According to green postmodernism, there aren’t any —all ranking judgments are bad. But again, that itself is a ranking judgment —it ranks nonranking as higher and better than ranking—and that itself is a major ranking. So once you see that ranking is unavoidable—and that is why you were doing so much of it, even when you claimed you weren’t—let’s look at what the basis of some good guidelines for ranking might be.

Now, this is tricky, because each level will have some quite different answers to this. So let’s even go with the major values of this level—the postmodern pluralistic, level 6—and see when ranking would actually be not only okay, but recommended, something to be encouraged, according to this level’s own values.

This level values equality above almost everything else. So let’s just notice that not all levels share this view. In fact, none of them do. Red power divides the world into predators and prey, and favors those things that help only itself, nobody else; absolutely zero equality here. Amber fundamentalism divides the world into the saved and the damned, saints and sinners, and values only those who accept the correct savior; all others are infidels, bound to burn eternally in hell; only true believers are equal. And orange divides the world into winners and losers, and values accomplishment, merit, and excellence above all others—none of this equality stuff. Only green values equality, and sees all people as being essential equal or egalitarian.

So let’s further notice this level is itself just that—a developmental level. It’s stage 6 in an overall developmental scheme. So the first judgment you can make based on these values is that vertical development—from archaic to

magic to power to mythic to rational to pluralistic—is much better than not having that development. In other words, those lower levels can be ranked and judged accordingly as being less valuable than this stage, level 6, because only this level believes in equality, and this level requires development. And the reason is that, as we have seen, each level of development is characterized by an increase in wholeness—each level has an identity that gets larger and larger, more inclusive and more inclusive—and you will treat people morally only if you identify with them. So red power treats only itself morally; amber ethnocentric treats all its religious brothers and sisters morally, but not all infidels. Orange modern expands rights to all humans, and begins to treat all humans fairly and morally, but notices that some people share this view, and some don’t, so it begins to judge people on how tolerant they are, or are not. And green pluralism takes this to its ultimate limit, where it goes overboard and simply treats all people equally whether they treat others that way or not; whereas it should say, based on its own values, that anything that helps a person get to this stage where they can wish to treat people equally is good; not reaching this stage of wholeness, and thus not treating all people fairly, is bad, is wrong, is less good. And that’s a truth that holds across the developmental spectrum—namely, each level is adequate and good for its circumstances, but each higher level is “more adequate” and thus in some ways “better”—increasingly more inclusive, more embracing, more whole, more compassionate, more moral, and more loving.

So take Carol Gilligan’s stages of female moral development. Gilligan became famous for proposing, in her book In a Different Voice, that men and women reason differently—men with an emphasis on hierarchy and autonomy, and women with an emphasis on relationship and belonging. Feminists who believed that all hierarchies are bad jumped on Gilligan’s argument that men—not women—think hierarchically, and used it to blame men (and the patriarchy) for most of humanity’s ills. But these feminists, and postmodernists in general, studiously ignored a second point that Gilligan made in that book: namely, that both men and women develop through the same 4 basic hierarchical stages (her term). In women, Gilligan named these hierarchical levels as follows: stage 1, or selfish—the woman cares only for herself (this is our egocentric); stage 2, or care—the woman extends care from only herself to groups (our ethnocentric); stage 3, or universal care— the woman extends care to all humans, regardless of race, color, sex, or creed (our worldcentric); and stage 4, which she called integrated—where women

and men integrate the other sex’s attitude (our integral). So women might indeed tend to think non-hierarchically, but women’s non-hierarchical thinking itself develops through 4 hierarchical stages. This simple fact, feminists (and postmodernists) still haven’t grasped.

And that means they’ve also missed another important fact: that we can’t just say that what Western cultures under patriarchy need are more feminine values—which is quite common to hear nowadays—because clearly we don’t need the first two stages of feminine values. We do not need more selfish/narcissistic and sexist/racist thinking—which are the first two (and largest set) of feminine values described by Gilligan. Even according to these postmodern theorists—including feminists—those values are killing us. The higher stages of development (Gilligan’s stages 3 and 4) are not “equally good”; they are better—they have more wholeness, are more inclusive, and therefore are more moral, more caring, more loving, more valuable (and less oppressive, less dominating). We need the feminine values from the higher stages—from worldcentric and integral (as well as masculine values from the same higher stages as well, it might be noted). That is a judgment that is true and good, and more people should begin thinking that way, because the more they do, the better human beings will be treated.

So be careful when you think that you are not being judgmental, when in fact you are. And then look at the type of judgments you make, and try to base them on these well-tested developmental facts. In summary, we can say that each developmental level is good, but each higher level is “more good”—more inclusive, more whole, more conscious, more moral, more caring (and research consistently shows exactly that). And if there are levels higher than level 6, then those levels would be even more “more good,” more valuable—and there are.

Before we get to those, notice that all of this points to yet another important fact that is almost always overlooked by postmodern pluralism: There are two types of hierarchies, not just one. There are dominator hierarchies, and there are growth hierarchies. Dominator hierarchies are indeed nasty (oppressive and dominating)—things like the caste system, or hierarchies in criminal organizations (where the higher the level you are at, the more people you can dominate and oppress). But growth hierarchies are exactly the opposite—the higher your level, the more inclusive, the more caring, the more loving and embracing (the less dominating and less oppressing) you are. All of the developmental models we are talking about

here (including the 6-to-8 stages of Growing Up in general) are growth hierarchies, just like Carol Gilligan’s. In fact, most hierarchies in nature are growth hierarchies—like atoms to molecules to cells to organisms. Each of those levels “transcends and includes” its predecessor, so it becomes more and more whole, more and more inclusive, more and more embracing. And higher levels don’t oppress lower levels, they embrace them, they love them. Molecules don’t hate atoms, or oppress atoms, or dominate atoms—they include them, they embrace them; if anything, they love them. So keep that in mind about the growth hierarchies that we are discussing. This will be particularly hard if you are basically at pluralistic stage 6, because stage 6 inherently hates all hierarchies, without really realizing what it’s doing. It’s part of its “performative contradiction”—it puts hierarchy on the lowest level of its hierarchy.

So if, by using this Integral overview, you spot this stage-6 hidden map in yourself, then put it under the bright light of mindfulness and laser it to death with awareness. What you want to be aware of, to apply mindfulness to, is the simple attitude of judgment—it can be negative judgment or it can be positive judgment; the point is simply to videotape the very act, the very feeling, of judging, of thinking or feeling that this is better than that. Now, the point we just made is that in some cases, at least in the relative manifest world, sometimes one thing is better than another, and that’s fine. But what we want to do here, with this mindfulness session, is “transcend and include” all judgment—so that means, for the “transcend” part, we will want to be letting go of judgment itself altogether (and “include” it by simply being directly aware of it)—but no identifying with it, condemning it, being one with it, negating it, or condoning it—just videotaping it, just applying feeling-awareness to this extraordinary activity of judging. This is BETTER than that . . . and just exactly WHAT does that feel like? Where is the feeling of judgment located? What color is it, what shape is it, what does it look like, what does it smell like, what does it feel like? Get this judging attitude firmly in awareness, and then simply videotape it, fully, completely, carefully. The net effect of this will be to actually accomplish what pluralism simply claims to do: to let go of judging altogether.

By now it may have started to become obvious that, just as at each previous stage where we have applied direct feeling-awareness to a quality, character, trait, value, or item in general, this mindfulness will indeed help make that subject object. That is, if we have indeed been previously identified

with that item—if it has been part of our subject, our actual self—then this mindfulness will help us dis-identify with it, help us let go of it, transcend it, and open us to the next higher—and more whole, more inclusive, more complete—item, whatever that might be. The point is, whatever that new item is, your system already knows it; it is already present, waiting to emerge, waiting for you to let go of your present identity and open yourself to the next-higher identity. When that openness, that transcendence, that letting go, is in place, then nature will take its course, as it were—the next-higher item, whatever that is, will of itself emerge, will naturally and spontaneously come to the fore, and you will identify with that new characteristic (although you won’t see it as an object—not yet, anyway)—that will become part of your new subject, your new self, your more inclusive and more loving and more conscious self.

And the same is most definitely true of judging. So whether you think all judging is bad, or whether you hold several items in strong judgment, doesn’t really matter, in a sense. By making your present subject object—whatever it is—the new, next-higher, more inclusive, and more conscious subject and self will naturally emerge. If your present judgments are somehow true and universal and genuinely good, they won’t go anywhere; you’ll become more aware of them, but you won’t change them fundamentally. If, however, there are higher judgments, higher types of judgments or better judgments or more loving and conscious judgments, available anywhere in your system (laid down in the entire human race by millions of years of evolution), then you will start to identify with those—they will become part of your new, higher, more inclusive, more whole, more conscious self and subject.

And guess what? This continues straight to God.

Level 7—(Turquoise) Integral

I earlier said that the green postmodern level of consciousness, or level 6, was the last major level to emerge in history or human evolution to date. And on any sort of large scale, that’s true. But beginning a few decades ago, developmentalists began noticing the rare emergence of an entirely different type of stage or level, one that was fundamentally and significantly different from anything that had emerged to date in all of history. All of the previous levels—up to and including green postmodern pluralism—believe that their truth and values are the only truth and values in the world; that all the others

are misguided, confused, infantile, or just plain wrong. But this newly emerging level believed that there was some significance in every single previous level, no exceptions. If nothing else, they are all steps in one’s overall growth and development, and without those steps, there would be no growth and development in the first place. Just as you can’t go from atoms to molecules to cells to organisms by skipping molecules, so every stage in development is crucial—they are all incredibly important and significant, and this new stage seemed to intuitively understand this. Clare Graves called the emergence of this level a “momentous leap,” where “a chasm of unbelievable depth of meaning is crossed,” and pointed out that nothing even vaguely like this had ever existed before in human history.

This indeed does seem to be a monumental, history-making emergence— and indeed has occurred only recently, but promises to be the single most important and most profound transformation in all of human history. Let’s have a brief look at that; it might likely spell your own higher future.

To emphasize the profound difference between this new level and the previous levels, the first 6 levels (infrared archaic, magenta impulsive, red power, amber conformist, orange rational, and green pluralistic) were all called “1st tier,” and these new levels (there seemed to be a few substages here) were called “2nd tier.” This is the same distinction that Maslow called “deficiency needs” (1st tier) and “Being needs” (2nd tier). The 1st-tier levels are partial, narrow, excluding, separative, and driven by deficiencies; 2nd-tier levels are inclusive, embracing, comprehensive, integral, and driven by abundance—and this was the first time in history that any sort of level of consciousness like this had ever emerged to a significant degree. It was still fairly rare—today, only around 5% of the worldwide population is at Integral levels of development. But they are game changers—they literally change our views of virtually everything they approach. Integral Theory itself is driven by these levels, or attempts to be. This overall general level—integral level 7 (given the color turquoise)—is referred to also as integrated, holistic, strategist, systemic, and so on, all names indicating its comprehensive nature. Integral is the most inclusive, the most sophisticated, the most complex, the most conscious, the most embracing, and includes the greatest numbers of perspectives, of any levels to emerge in all of history, and truly marks a “momentous leap in meaning.” It is the first level in all of human evolution that believes all other levels have some importance, while those levels themselves believe only they are important. This is truly integral, inclusive,

comprehensive, all-embracing. If you have a fair amount of this overall integral level in you, first and

foremost you will be driven by wholeness. You will tend to seek out Big Pictures and Big Data. You want to know how things fit together, how they all have some place in this wondrous Kosmos. In any argument, you will tend to think not that one side is totally right and the other side totally wrong, but that both sides have some important portion of the truth, and that a larger view embracing them both could be found. You will see connections everywhere, believing that everything is related to and interwoven with everything else in some deep way. Knowledge is not divided into dozens and dozens of separate, fragmented, siloed, isolated disciplines, but is somehow all interwoven into a holistic tapestry and dynamic meshwork. Relationships will replace isolated things, and networks start to jump out everywhere. You will see the world populated not by hundreds of separate races but by one glorious, all-embracing, universal humanity, albeit with plenty of individual and cultural differences. But there is a unity-in-diversity that speaks directly to you.

Personally, at this level the self-esteem needs give way to the self-actualization needs (à la Maslow)—the needs of the greater individual Essence, emerging with all of its extraordinary potentials, of greater creativity, greater awareness, greater inclusiveness, greater love and caring, and more phenomenal capacities in general, including being at least 10 times more efficient than any 1st-tier level. Ten times! You might find yourself going to numerous transformation workshops, seminars, weekend training sessions (you’ll find most of them dominated by green, but you’ll likely keep looking); you might take up meditation, yoga, t’ai chi. You might develop an interest in cybertech and software design, but drawn by the Net’s capacity to form almost endless networks of increasing wholeness. If you enter business and rise to a position of leadership, it will be because you see the company-as-a-whole as its greatest asset, and seek to help all employees (and all stakeholders) use work as a means of self-realization and self-fulfillment, not merely a way to make money. As soon as you see any growth hierarchies— such as this 8-stage one—they will make immediate sense to you, and you will file them away in memory, at least loosely, ready to draw on them as needed. If you’re on a spiritual (not religious) path—which we’ll discuss more in a moment—and you happen to have a partner, you will want that partner to be following the path with you, so you both can share fully in its

many challenges and rewards, soul mates seeking joint Enlightenment—“I am the Buddha we are.” If you’re raising kids, you will indeed want them to feel special, but by helping them learn to connect that specialness with actual accomplishments in the world, not by merely telling them they’re special for no reason at all (which is basically just a form of lying to them). You’ll get very tired of and even bored with the two-party system, finding that it is altogether too narrow and partial a view, looking instead for approaches that include the best and brightest aspects of both parties, and candidates who seem to embody that holistic view. Both thinking and feeling will be important to you, not just feeling (as with pluralistic) and not just thinking (as with rational). Thinking and feeling are actually, for the first time in development, brought together and tightly integrated at this stage, so that both the head and the heart become equally important (as the psychologist John Broughton’s research found, at this stage “mind and body are both experiences of an integrated self”).4 You will take a natural interest in all things global, including wicked problems like global warming, but (unlike postmodernism) instead of absolutizing the biosphere, you will see it as part of an even larger network that includes things like the noosphere, or the realm of ideas, integrated with the biosphere, or realm of life, and the pneumosphere, or realm of spirit.

If you have a good deal of this stage in you, you will value wholeness above virtually all other qualities. So, in the mindfulness portion, simply focus awareness on this feeling or notion of wholeness. Where do you see this wholeness? In yourself, in your culture, in the world itself, in Gaia or the planetary ecosystem, in the solar system, the galaxy, the entire universe itself? How does it feel in all the various areas that you see as whole? Focus on the very feeling of wholeness itself, wherever it appears. Again, what does it look it, feel like, smell like; what size is it, what shape is it, what color is it; where is it located? Videotape the sensation, the idea, the feeling of wholeness from every angle imaginable. Really bring it into awareness as an object, prying it out of your subjective identity and opening your awareness to even higher emergents. You will always have access to being aware of wholeness—you will just no longer be exclusively identified with that feeling. You will transcend and include it into whatever next-higher level you inhabit.

In the meantime, the Integral level is seen and understood as the very leading edge of evolution itself. (Because evolution is unceasing, there will

always be future, yet higher levels of wholeness and awareness and inclusiveness—and we’ll mention a few possible ones in a moment—but for all practical purposes, the turquoise Integral is the highest level a human can reasonably be expected to reach; it is the pragmatic pinnacle of evolution to date. (And here we are treating all substages as part of one, general, overall Integral level, or “2nd tier.”) Higher possible levels represent less than 1/10 of 1% of the population. We’ll mention them in a moment, simply because, if you are at Integral, you will be open to moving into a yet higher level— which altogether we call “Super-Integral” or “3rd tier.” In the meantime, since each level of development is “more whole” or “more integral” than its predecessor—and this turquoise level is the generally highest expectable level to date—then it itself is usually just called “the” Integral level.)

Another way to say that this Integral level is essentially the highest, leading-edge stage of evolution to date is to say that the holons at this leading edge are the holons with the most depth in the entire Kosmos—that is, are the most significant. (“Span” means the number of holons on a particular level; “depth” means the number of levels in a particular holon. Each stage in evolution produces greater depth, less span. Thus, molecules have more depth than atoms—for one thing, they include atoms, so of course they have more depth; but there are, and always will be, fewer molecules—less span—than atoms. Same with molecules and cells, cells and organisms, and so on.) So the more fundamental a holon—the lower it is on a holarchy—then the more span it has and the less depth. The more significant a holon—the higher on a holarchy—then the less span and the more depth. Thus, to say it again from a slightly different angle, atoms have little depth; depending on what you count as a “level” of being, an atom only has a few levels—perhaps strings, quarks, and subatomic particles. But atoms have enormous span—there are many more atoms than there are molecules or cells or organisms, because each of those stages contains numerous atoms itself. So each higher level in a holarchy always has fewer holons than its predecessor—there will always be fewer molecules than there are atoms; always fewer cells than molecules; always fewer organisms than cells—and there are no exceptions to that anywhere; it’s physically impossible. So as the depth increases—molecules have more depth, more levels within them, than atoms (at the very least, they include atoms and so add one more level of depth than atoms themselves have); and cells have more depth, more levels, than molecules—there is less span, since there will always be fewer cells than molecules; and so on. In

general, as noted, evolution produces greater depth, less span. And thus, by the time we get to a human holon at the turquoise Integral

level, we have a holon with the most depth (and the least span) of any other holon anywhere in the Kosmos. Remember, as we earlier noted, by the time a human being appears in evolution (and with evolution measured as a one-year length, humans appear in the last minute of the last day), because they are an exclamation mark to evolution thus far, a human being actually and literally contains in itself holons from every major level of evolution to date: we saw that a human being literally contains or enfolds quarks, subatomic particles, atoms, molecules, prokaryotic cells, eukaryotic cells, organ systems that include the fundamentals of photosynthetic biochemistry, the neural nets of early animals, the spinal cords of fish and amphibians, the brain stem of reptiles, the limbic system of paleomammals (e.g., horses), the cortex of primates, and capped by its own neocortex—the human being “transcends and includes” every major holon ever produced by evolution in its 14-billion-year history. And then humans themselves begin evolving—and their stages of evolution or development are likewise holons, with each stage transcending-and-including its predecessor, so that these stages inherently become even more and more whole, more and more inclusive, more and more conscious, more and more loving, more and more moral. And by the time we reach turquoise Integral, we have a stage that has transcended and included every single level produced by evolution in the entire history of the total Kosmos. Humans at this turquoise Integral stage are, in other words, holons with the greatest depth that has ever appeared in the course of the entire manifest world in its entire 14-billion-year history.

And this leading edge of evolution is beginning, in humans, to remake and refashion every single area, event, and item that it touches. Wholeness, real inclusiveness, actual embrace, genuine fullness, total outreach, are showing up in every major human activity in every major area worldwide. It is at the leading edge of science and medicine; the leading edge of art and the humanities; the leading edge of politics and governance systems; the leading edge of business and economics; the leading edge of spiritual Growing Up; and the leading edges of Growing Up in general. It is indeed an unbelievable and momentous leap across a chasm of meaning; and it will never leave the world the same as it once was.

And since you have read this far, it is very likely that you—you—are at this leading edge as well.

If so, we noted, you will particularly hunger for wholeness. You will want to see how everything is connected to everything else; you will want to find and see the patterns that connect; you will long to find your own life inherently one with Life itself, one with the Kosmos in general, unified and united in its very being. Let this desire for wholeness come fully into awareness; look at it, feel it, see it, be aware of it. Allow this desire for wholeness—and the feeling of wholeness itself—to fill your awareness, flood your consciousness. Videotape wholeness front to back, side to side, top to bottom. And by making the present state of wholeness an object, you are simply creating an opening or clearing for yet-higher forms of wholeness to emerge and cascade through your being. By making present wholeness object, you are simply opening to yet-greater forms of wholeness lying in your future.

Which brings us to the next possible stage of Growing Up.

Level 8—(White) 3rd-Tier Super-Integral

Now, although the Integral level is essentially the very leading edge of today’s development and evolution, there are actually still more levels on their way—for the simple reason that there are no signs that evolution stops, or will ever stop. Accounts vary as to how many truly higher levels there might be, and exactly what their characteristics are. But given the nature of evolution—namely, it is an endless affair, constantly going beyond what went before—we would certainly expect to see evolution continue into the future. And we would expect that future levels of evolution would still be marked by many of the same characteristics that have marked each level of evolution so far—that is, each higher level transcends and includes its predecessor, is therefore more whole, more inclusive, more conscious, with a wider identity, and thus more caring, more loving, more embracing. These higher possible levels are generically referred to as 3rd-tier or Super-Integral levels (collectively, level 8, color “clear light” or “white”).

The evidence that these higher levels exist is strong; exactly what they are is less obvious. And remember, we don’t get much help here from the Traditions,5 because they were generally unaware of structures and structure-stages themselves—they focused on Waking Up, not on the higher stages of Growing Up, which, again, were only discovered a century ago—and thus they tended to track all of their higher stages in terms of “states.” But based

on evidence from visionaries such as Sri Aurobindo—who was trained in Western modes of evolutionary thinking and thus had at least some sense of structure-stages—as well as various types of early research, I believe we can postulate up to 4 major 3rd-tier stages, which I call indigo para-mind, violet meta-mind, ultraviolet overmind, and white supermind. I do want to emphasize that the sum total of individuals at any of these 3rd-tier structure-stages today is, I would estimate, considerably less than 1/10 of 1%. If you are at “the” Integral stage, you are pragmatically at the leading edge; and, of course, as such, you will be open to the emergence of 3rd-tier levels as you continue your growth and development. If you would like to track some of these details, please see my forthcoming book, The Religion of Tomorrow. But you will have all you pragmatically need for your own growth and development by paying attention to the stages that we have presented thus far; and what I’ll say about Super-Integral stages is enough to orient you to what you might look for if you are today riding the leading edge. Remember, if these higher stages are indeed present, they will know what to do when it is time for them to begin appearing in you, because they will emerge naturally and spontaneously—the whole point about stages is that they are real levels of real reality, not just some idea that somebody somewhere dreamt up. So, at the edge of turquoise Integral, simply practice Integral Mindfulness and hold yourself open to any and all future developments, which most assuredly will occur precisely as they are supposed to. . . . And this will be accelerated by the state practice of Waking Up, which we will get to next.

And thus, if you are now at an Integral level of development, then you are, for all real purposes, on the very edge of evolution itself, and your thoughts, feelings, and actions will actually be co-creating the future levels that humans will eventually be inhabiting on the whole. Thus, welcome to your place in history! (We’ll see shortly that that has an actual, real meaning in evolutionary terms.)

This whole process of laying down new levels of being and awareness is part of the general nature of evolution or development itself. For whatever reason, the universe that we know—at least as we know it now—is a universe in which each moment inherently transcends and includes its predecessor. For Alfred North Whitehead, for example, each moment comes to be as a “subject of experience” or a “drop of experience.” And as the new subject comes into being, it “prehends” (his term, which basically means “touches” or “feels”) the previous subject, thus making it object. The prehension, or

“touching,” of the previous moment by this present moment constitutes the past’s influence on the present. Obviously, if you touch and embrace an object, that object will affect you—and that’s what happens as each moment touches and embraces (prehends) the previous moment (which itself had touched and embraced its previous moment, and so on indefinitely). This is the “causative” or “determining” moment of the past on the present. If this were all that there was, this would be a purely deterministic, mechanistic universe, with no creativity, novelty, or innovation possible (except for fluke “mutations” or aberrations).

But, according to Whitehead (and I agree), each moment, in addition to prehending the previous moment, adds its own bit of novelty or newness or creativity. It not only includes the past, it transcends it. It not only prehends the previous subject (making it object of the new subject), it adds a bit of emergent novelty to the new subject, thus introducing a measure of freedom and newness into the sequence. Now, if the holons thus unfolding have very little depth (such as atoms), then the degree of novelty they can add is very small, and their temporal unfolding will appear very much deterministic, or ruled by strict cause-and-effect. But, Whitehead adds, “little novelty” is not the same as “no novelty.” After all, atoms managed to give rise, eventually, to molecules, a very creative advance indeed. And molecules managed an astonishing leap of creativity—at one point, dozens of very complex molecules managed to find themselves in the same vicinity; they joined together, a cell wall dropped around them, and out of their own inherent creative drive, life appeared! An actual living cell out of molecules! The point is that creative novelty is built into the very fabric of the universe—and that creative novelty is what ultimately drives evolution (this creative drive has been known as everything from “self-organization” to “Eros” to “Love” to “Spirit-in-action”). This is why evolution was already operating beginning with the Big Bang itself and moving forward—it did not have to wait for the emergence of life, sexuality, nucleic acids, random mutations, and natural selection in order to get started. Those were just some particular stages in this ongoing transcend-and-include, or “self-transcendence through self-organization,” which was at the heart of evolution itself all the way back to the very first moments of the Big Bang. (And that is why some people think of evolution as “Spirit-in-action,” which I happen to think is a fine idea. In any event, Eros or creativity or evolution was operating in the universe from the very beginning, or there never would have appeared anything new at all.)

But notice in this evolutionary unfolding the crucial notion that we see again: the subject of this moment becomes the object of the subject of the next. That is the very form of transcend-and-include that we see in everything from atoms to stages of human development. And that is why a drive to increasing wholeness, increasing inclusiveness, increasing consciousness, increasing love and care and concern and embrace, is inherently built into the universe as we know it. It’s the only way one moment can fit with the next. No other arrangement will work; and thus in every single area of the universe that we look, we find evolution “transcending and including” with its ever-emerging elements.

And this means that as you yourself practice Integral Mindfulness—which transcends and includes each moment, and does so moment to moment—you are aligning yourself with the very drive, force, and process of evolution itself. And if you want to see evolution as “Spirit-in-action,” then you are aligning yourself with Spirit itself, you are aligning yourself with God’s will, you are acting as God is acting in this manifest universe moment to moment.

But whatever you might think of that, one of the most fascinating aspects of evolution and development is the actual form or pattern of the holons that are created by evolution. Because despite the bravura of natural science claiming to understand all of this perfectly, science really has no idea exactly how “morphogenesis” (the creation of form or development itself) comes about. For example, when there is an attempt to synthesize a new and long-chain protein for the first time, even if various laboratories around the world are working on it, it can take quite some time. But when one laboratory manages to do so, within a very short period all the other laboratories tend to also do the same thing, independently. And what’s more astonishing, even though this protein could fold into literally thousands of ways—and there is absolutely nothing contained anywhere in the protein itself to indicate which way it should fold—once it has folded into a particular form, the proteins being synthesized anywhere else in the world will fold into the identical form or pattern. This “form” is being stored someplace that is very real, but it is nowhere in the protein itself. Where on earth is it?

Science has no answer. Some of the ancient traditions supply at least believable hypotheses, most of them centered on the notion of what the Mahayana Buddhist Lankavatara Sutra calls the “storehouse consciousness,” the repository of all past forms anywhere in the universe (somewhat like the “Akashic records” of theosophy). But developmental sequences everywhere

have these patterns or forms, and nobody knows how or where they are stored, but they show up everywhere. In embryology, for example, if you have a growing tadpole, and you remove a portion of the cells that are growing where the tail is, and a portion growing where the head is, and switch them, then remarkably, the tail section cells put in the head location will develop normally into a head; and the head cells put into the tail section will grow into a normal tail. There is nothing in any of the cells that perfectly governs this—rather, there is some “overall form” stored somewhere that is itself governing the very shape of what is developing. (This “somewhere” is often called a “morphogenetic field”—morph- meaning “form,” and -genetic meaning “development.” The term was invented by the well-known embryologist C. H. Waddington to help explain this mystery—it was a morphogenetic field that was governing the form of development.)

The same is true of the form of these stages of Growing Up. Take, for example, the amber Mythic stage of development. Humans historically began developing this stage because the human holon not only prehended its previous stages, but also kept adding a little bit of novelty—until finally, an entirely new stage suddenly jumped into existence; and just as remarkable, people around the world, getting ready for a new developmental stage, all began developing into the same form of the Mythic stage. That “form” had been stored somewhere—very likely, the same place that the protein-folding form and the tadpole-developing form and all the other “morphogenetic forms” are stored—and wherever that is, it is a very real, very actual, very influential place, and it keeps reaching down and guiding the form of developing holons everywhere in the universe. And as new holons emerge— due to transcend-and-include—those new forms are likewise stored in that remarkable Kosmic storage bin of form.

Your Literal Place in History

Right now, the Integral stage of form-development is right in the process of itself being formed. And that means that individuals’ actions at that leading edge are being stored in the Kosmic storage bin, and will help to shape and create the very form of the Integral level as it finally appears for all subsequent human beings—just as the Magic form, laid down some 150,000 years ago, is still the identical form that will be experienced today at stage 2 by every newborn child the world over, bar none.

And thus, to repeat a comment I have made elsewhere: “Every time you think an Integral thought; every time you conceive an Integral idea; every time your pulse quickens with the thought of a more beautiful, more truthful, more ethical world tomorrow; every time you read and study, or create and write, of Integral notions; every time you even ask, ‘What can I do to bring this about, to speed this up?’; every time you dream the dream of a more inclusive tomorrow, the dream of a more harmonious future, the dream of a more balanced and cherished Earth, the dream of a Spirituality that touches the God in each and every being alive, and gives that God an embodied home in your own being; every time you reach out for a future that is even just a little more Whole than the one today; every time you imagine any human activity—from education to parenting to medicine to government to law— redrawn in a more inclusive and Integral fashion; every time you look into the eyes of a young child, perhaps even your own, and wish for them a future of greater love and compassion and care and concern, and see them smile in the radiant halo of that embracing tomorrow; every time you think a moment a little more Whole than the previous one, or see partialities brought together in the patterns that connect, or reach out to a future where all God’s children are judged in Kosmic terms, not parochial or prejudiced ones; every time you make a choice that is in favor of the betterment of humankind and all living beings in their entirety; every time you see broken pieces and fractured shards and torn and tortured human beings brought together in a more unified and inclusive and caring embrace; and every time you yearn for a tomorrow even slightly more unified and inclusive and embracing than today—every time, every single time, you do anything like any of those, you are yourself directly, immediately, and irrevocably building interior Integral objects that are instantly being stored in that real Kosmic storage bin, adding a few inches to the size of that tsunami racing in our direction now.”6

And that is why, when I said, “Welcome to your place in history,” I meant it literally. Welcome to your place in history! You will literally be part of the thoughts and actions of every human being born henceforth, from now until the end of the world.

“Kosmos” is a Greek word meaning the entire world—the physical, the emotional, the mental, and the spiritual. In one of the major uses in Integral

Theory, “Kosmocentric” is a general stage-realm of development: “all-world-centered,” including not just all humans but all sentient beings, all of reality itself. 2. Personal communication with Dr. Roger Walsh, May 2014. Dr. Walsh is a prominent researcher in consciousness studies and related fields. 3. We have coined “its” as the plural of “it,” though of course “they” and “them” are customary for 3rd-person plural. 4. John M. Broughton, “The Development of Natural Epistemology in Adolescence and Early Adulthood,” unpublished doctoral dissertation (Cambridge: Harvard University, 1975). 5. By the term “Traditions” or the “Great Traditions” I mean primarily what are known as the world religions as well as their esoteric forms—such as Judaism and Kabbalah; Christianity and its mysticism; Islam and Sufism; Hinduism, Vedanta, Kashmir Shaivism, and Yoga; Buddhism’s various schools; Taoism; neo-Confucianism, and so on. 6. The Fourth Turning: Imagining the Evolution of an Integral Buddhism (Boston: Shambhala Publications, 2014).

Waking Up: The Steps to Enlightenment

So, we have just summarized an overview of the 8 major levels of growth, development, and evolution recognized by most of the world’s developmental models. You can see that each level or stage really inhabits its own world, so to speak—it sees different phenomena, it has different needs and drives, it holds very different values, it has very different types of identity, it has a very different sense of morality or what is right, and it holds different truths, or what it considers to be ultimately real. You can use this Integral overview to help you spot which level or stage you are at, for the most part. Once you have used the Integral overview to do that, you can then use mindfulness to bring that level into focus, to become fully aware of it, to see it as an object—to look at it, and thus stop using it as something to look through at the world. Look at it, not through it. Simply hold it in mind from every possible viewpoint: What does it look like? What size is it? What color is it? Where in the body is it located? What does it feel like? “What does it look like from the side, from the back, from the front, from the top, from the bottom? What things can you find that particularly trigger it? How long can you remember being at this stage? How many things in your life have been built by this particular stage?

The general goal, of course, is to transform to higher and higher levels of consciousness, until you reach the very leading edge of today’s evolution— which, today, is around level 7, the turquoise Integral level. That is the highest level that you can reasonably expect to reach (although once there, you will naturally be spontaneously open to the Super-Integral levels). But the Integral level is the most whole, the most inclusive, the most all-embracing level of consciousness generally available in today’s world. Reaching that level means, in essence, that the person doing so is as Grown Up as they can possibly be at this point in our evolution (and, of course, individuals can be at different levels in each of their lines or multiple intelligences—as we will explore in detail later on—so the growth in all lines needs to be kept in mind; the Integral level is the highest level in any and all of the lines).

But there is another path of development that, ironically, is ignored by virtually every single one of the modern developmental models. Hardly one of them even refers to it. But this axis of development—that of Waking Up— is found throughout the premodern world, and is the most widespread map of development by far in premodern cultures. Arthur Lovejoy, in his brilliant lectures collected in The Great Chain of Being, maintained that this map or model was held by the largest number of sensitive minds (East and West, I add) throughout the largest portion of our history. And it formed the core— the often central but secret or esoteric core (not shared widely with the general public)—of virtually every one of the world’s great religions, even those known mostly for their narrative versions. These “esoteric” paths, the Paths of Waking Up, or the Paths of the Great Liberation, were present in Judaism as Kabbalah and Hasidism; in Christianity as its numerous contemplative branches (and well-known figures such as St. John of the Cross, St. Teresa of Ávila, Meister Eckhart, and the Rhineland mystics); in Islam as the extraordinary variety of Sufi orders; and of course in all of the major Eastern meditative traditions, many of which are still quite active today, from Zen to Tibetan Buddhism to Vedanta to Kashmir Shaivism (a Hindu tantric tradition) to contemplative Taoism to Neo-Confucianism to various schools of Yoga. Mindfulness itself is part of this grand wisdom Tradition.

But the stages traced and mapped by these great Traditions are quite different from the stages in the modern Western models of Growing Up. The Growing Up models map out what we called structures of consciousness, and what marks all structures is that they are held largely unconsciously—they are, indeed, hidden maps, because even in the midst of using one of these various maps, we have no idea that we are. Again, they are like hidden grammar rules, which we obediently obey every day but with no idea we are doing so. And that’s why these hidden maps were only discovered about 100 years ago, with the advent of modern developmental psychology.

So why are the stages of Waking Up not included in these maps of Growing Up? Well, like the stages of Growing Up, the stages of Waking Up are also hidden—but they are hidden for a totally different reason and in a totally different way. They are hidden, not because they are almost impossible to see by looking within (as structures are); they are hidden because the means of achieving them—the paths taken to achieve them—are very rare, and when taken, require a considerable amount of time, effort, and

energy to pursue. But once you are following a particular path and you go through one of its stages (one of the stages of Waking Up), those stages are easily and immediately noticed. When you’re in one of those states, you definitely know it. (If you have an experience of a universal love and being one with the entire universe, you will clearly know it!)

These stages of Waking Up are totally different from the stages of Growing Up, where you don’t need a special path to reach them—just keep growing and you’ll naturally go through all 8 of those stages, with no special work at all. But even when you’re at a Growing Up stage and fully using it, you have no idea that you are.

But the stages of Waking Up—once you reach them—are utterly obvious. They’re less common, but only because the paths of reaching them are less common. And they’re less common because they’re fairly hard, they take work, they take dedication, they take effort and usually several years to achieve. So, as Western researchers began studying various stages of development, they rarely if ever found somebody at a higher stage of Waking Up, because those stages are not normally given as part of a human organism’s typical growth and development, the way stages of Growing Up are. Rather, they have to be taken up as a voluntary, extra step during life, and if you don’t undertake them intentionally and on purpose, you’ll likely never reach them. They don’t just happen normally, as stages of Growing Up do.

On the other hand, paths of Waking Up—although they developed numerous, very effective methods of moving through the stages of Waking Up—none of them discovered any of the stages of Growing Up, even though they were moving through them (as all humans do), because, again, you can’t see these stages simply by looking within. You can sit on your Zen mat for 20 years, and you’ll never see a thought that says, “This is a stage-4 Mythic thought” or “This is a stage-5 moral thought,” just as you can sit on your Zen mat indefinitely and never see the rules of grammar you follow daily. So, very strangely, it turned out that none of the Western developmental models have stages of Waking Up, and none of the meditative systems, East or West, have stages of Growing Up. These two incredibly important and profound developmental streams have never been brought together and practiced together until just recently (when approaches such as Integral Theory began doing so). It’s rather astonishing that these two paths have remained separate and isolated for virtually all of humanity’s history. It’s outrageous, actually.

Because what it means is that humanity has never been whole. It has been a broken humanity through virtually all of its history—and its history has been exactly that: a history of brokenness.

But here we bring the two paths together and give both an equal emphasis.

States of Consciousness

So, to repeat, in chapter 1 we took a short tour of the 8 major levels of Growing Up. The major paths of Waking Up, by contrast, all tend to focus on 4 or 5 major natural states of consciousness, and these natural states—when entered consciously—become the general stages in the path of Waking Up; we call these “state-stages” to distinguish them from the “structure-stages” of Growing Up. Variations on these state-stages exist around the world, but they commonly include the waking state, the dream state, the deep dreamless state, the empty witnessing state, and the pure nondual or unity state. Particularly as these become stages of meditation, they are often referred to, respectively, as the physical (or what is also referred to as “gross”) state, the subtle state, the very subtle or causal state, the turiya or witnessing state, and turiyatita, the nondual or unity or Enlightened state (to give the version common to Vedanta Hinduism, Kashmir Shaivism, and Vajrayana Buddhism, although also found in Western schools such as Neoplatonism, Sufism, and Kabbalah).

Thus, Evelyn Underhill, in her classic study Mysticism, maintained that all Western mystics go through essentially the same 4 or 5 major stages of unfolding: usually following a preliminary awakening experience, they move through a gross purification, a subtle illumination, a dark night or infinite abyss, and a unity consciousness—or gross, subtle, causal, unity. In the Eastern system of Mahamudra Buddhism, the major stages are sometimes referred to as: “identifying our gross state, then realizing directly that state; identifying our subtle state, then realizing directly that state; identifying our very subtle (or causal) state, then realizing that state directly”; with the ground of all of them being ever-present nondual Awakened awareness— again, gross, subtle, causal, and nondual unity.

Daniel P. Brown, the Harvard psychologist who was one of my coauthors in Transformations of Consciousness, began his study of the stages of meditation by looking at 14 root texts of a school of Tibetan Buddhism. After identifying 6 major stages in all of those texts, he compared them with the Buddhist Abhidharma system, Patanjali’s Yoga Sutras, and writings of

several Chinese and Western schools, and found an essentially similar pattern of these 6 major stages. Also in Transformations of Consciousness, we included Harvard theologian John Chirban’s study of a half-dozen early desert saints of Christianity, and found 5 or 6 contemplative stages they all went through—and these were highly similar to Brown’s (and Underhill’s and others’) overall maps of Waking Up: all variations on gross, subtle, causal, witnessing, and nondual unity.

As a final, quick example of many that could be given, the postmodern American adept Adi Da maintains, “To Realize Most Perfect Divine Enlightenment, the ego must be transcended through three distinct phases— first at the \[gross\] physical level (the level of ‘money, food, and sex’), then at the subtle level (the level of internal visions, auditions, and all kinds of mystical experience), and finally at the causal level (the root-level of conscious existence, wherein the sense of ‘I’ and ‘other,’ or the subject-object dichotomy, seems to arise in Consciousness).”1 The fourth phase for Adi Da is the Realization of “Always-Already Truth,” the ever-present nondual Goal, Ground, and Condition of all existence, high or low, sacred or profane, manifest or unmanifest. Thus, gross, subtle, causal (implicit root-witnessing), and nondual. These same 4 or 5 major states of consciousness keep showing up in meditation system after meditation system, worldwide and throughout history.

Part of the reason that there is such a similarity among these Paths of Waking Up might have to do with the fact that many of the natural states of consciousness that are entered with pure awareness have roots in direct biological brain-wave patterns, and those are similar wherever humans appear —all humans, for example, wake, dream, and sleep, and those patterns seem to be universally similar (trying to claim, à la postmodernist extreme “cultural constructivism,” that Jews and Hindus have different waking, dreaming, and deep-sleep brain states just doesn’t wash). Entering those states in full awareness, then, would be expected to produce equally similar results for whoever did so—thus the cross-culturally similar gross, subtle, and causal state-stages of the Paths of Waking Up (and they all maintain, of course, that all of those states are grounded in the groundless Ground or Nondual Being of ever-present unity consciousness, which would therefore also likely be similar, in deep features, worldwide, even though all of them would—and do —differ in surface features. Thus, for example, the deep features of the brain-wave patterns in the dream state are the same for humans everywhere,

although the dream contents differ from culture to culture and individual to individual).

Figure 2.1 is a simple diagram representing these 5 major states or state-stages of the general path of Waking Up as they essentially appear cross-culturally.2 Again, these are basic deep features of the general commonality of these states, fully realizing that their surface features usually vary to some significant degree from culture to culture and often individual to individual. The 5 major states are shown in bold; Underhill’s stages of Western mysticism are shown in the left-hand area (gross purification, subtle illumination, causal dark night, nondual unification); stages of Highest Yoga Tantra, or anuttara-tantra (typical of Eastern versions), are listed at the bottom right area (5 skandhas and 80 mental conceptions of the gross state, white appearance and red increase of the subtle state, and black \[formless, dreamless\] near-attainment of the causal). And three “dark nights” are listed, representing the trials and turmoils of the self-system—the “Total Self” or consciousness as a whole—as it continues to transcend and include its lesser states by breaking an exclusive identity with a lower state and shifting its identity to the next higher—with all the bliss, fear, and torture typically involved.

Figure 2.1. Major stages of meditative states. (To view this figure in a larger format, visit www.shambhala.com/integralmeditation.)

But in general, this diagram summarizes that second, major type of human growth and development that we all have available to us. In addition to the growth of structures of consciousness (Growing Up), we have available the possibility of growth through states of consciousness (Waking Up). And this means we have two major, but quite different, types of spiritual growth and development as well: a growth through structure-stages (spiritual Growing Up, the movement of the spiritual line of development through the 6-to-8

major levels of development), and a growth through state-stages (a growth of consciousness through the 5 or so major states of awareness—gross/waking, subtle/dreaming, causal/deep sleep, empty witnessing, and pure nondual “unity”)—all laid down by evolution’s unyielding and unrelenting transcend-and-include. All of those deep features are often cross-culturally the same wherever we find them, but their surface features vary from culture to culture, and often individual to individual.

Now what we’re going to do here is particularly focus on the two highest states in this overall sequence—the empty Witness and pure nondual Unity— simply because everybody is familiar enough with the first three stages: gross/waking, subtle/dreaming, and causal/deep sleep. But even though, in a certain special sense (which we will see), these two highest states are “ever-present,” many people are not consciously aware of them. And yet they are, basically, the two most important states, since they are responsible for the various forms of “Waking Up” or “Enlightenment” or “Awakening”—these two states are end sequences of the entire Waking Up series.

So I’ll first briefly run through the first three states—just to see what we’re talking about here—and then we will jump to the last two stages, the two highest stages in the sequence, and set out a series of experiential exercises to catch a direct glimpse of each, so we can see what’s involved—and also, how these differ dramatically from any of the levels of Growing Up we’ve already discussed. The names of the stages in both sequences don’t even sound similar—archaic, magic, mythic, rational, pluralistic, and integral sound nothing like gross waking, subtle dreaming, causal deep sleep, pure witnessing, and nondual unity. And they aren’t similar—they are very different sequences through considerably different realms, and you can be highly developed in one and poorly developed in the other. The ideal goal for the Integral approach, of course, is to be fully developed in both, and that is what Integral awareness aims for—the highest level or structure (in today’s world, the Integral level) in the highest state (nondual unity)—or the best of both possible worlds.

Gross, Subtle, and Causal States

The gross, subtle, and causal realms, as we noted, seem universally so similar because in most cases the brain patterns of each are similar in humans wherever they appear. The waking state, the dream state, and the deep

dreamless state are essentially similar in humans worldwide (the dream state, for example, is 4–8 cycles of brain waves in humans everywhere). But rarely do people actually experience all these states in full consciousness, and therefore the deepest, hidden “secrets” of these states remain just that— hidden. Again, early developmental researchers found few people at these stages because so few people had actually taken up a training that would expose them to these states in a conscious, aware fashion. Because, to repeat, what we have generally found is that in any overall meditation sequence, as these major natural states are entered in consciousness, they become actual stages in the overall meditative path (i.e., they are state-stages).

The general idea is that Awareness per se, or Consciousness, or Wakefulness, starts out in a human being identified with the gross physical world—Wakefulness is identified with the waking state. And if Wakefulness is trained, it can enter successively the dream state, the deep dreamless state, the empty Witness (or “True Self”) state, and the ultimate, nondual, “unity” state—a completely “Awakened Wakefulness.” But in the meantime, not only the stages of Waking Up, but all the stages of Growing Up, all start out in the sensorimotor, physical or physiological, gross realm. Wakefulness is identified with the waking state, and that’s all it can directly recognize in full awareness.

Most of the Great Traditions identify the gross or physical state with insentient matter—that is, with things that are not alive, such as rocks and metal. (Of course, virtually all of the Traditions maintain that even “lifeless” matter is a form or manifestation of Life, Mind, or Spirit itself—and thus “matter” doesn’t lack Spirit, it’s simply the “lowest” level of Spirit.) But in any event, the gross or physical realm is the realm of sensorimotor matter— all of those material objects, items, and processes that you easily see with your senses everywhere around you. You are already conscious of this realm, this state, in your ordinary waking awareness. And your self is already identified with items from this realm—your physical body, physical food, physical needs and desires (all the infrared Archaic items we have already seen).

Now individuals will always be displaying some degree of growth in both developmental sequences—some degree of Growing Up and some degree of Waking Up. Everybody starts at square one in both sequences—so as a newborn, your fixed starting identity is with the first stage of Growing Up (the infrared Archaic) and the “lowest” state/realm of Waking Up (the

gross/waking). We call this dual-growth situation the “dual center of gravity,” because each person will always have a particular degree of growth in each sequence (their “dual centers”). So they will have a general or average level of development in structures or Growing Up (this is their “structure center of gravity”), and they will have a general or average level of development in states or Waking Up (this is their “state center of gravity”). So a person could be (magenta, gross), or (orange, gross), or (amber, subtle), or (integral, causal), or (integral, nondual unity), and so on.

Now many people, particularly in cultures that have very little awareness of the paths of Waking Up, can remain largely oriented to the gross/physical realm for their entire lives (even as they move to higher and higher levels of Growing Up). Others, particularly in cultures with premodern traditions still available, can reach the highest states of development available (awakened nondual “unity”) and yet still be at, say, ethnocentric amber in Growing Up (we already talked about some meditation teachers being highly developed in states but still poorly developed in structures).

But what will happen if you take up meditation, and you are presently identified with a level of Growing Up that also happens, let’s say, to still be in the gross/physical state—so you might be (mythic, gross) or (rational, gross) or (pluralistic, gross)—which basically means that you will be fully conscious only when your Wakefulness is in the waking state (so, again, your dual center of gravity might be \[amber, gross\] or \[orange, gross\] or \[green, gross\])—and so what will happen is that as you start to make the phenomena in your awareness into objects (as you practice something like mindfulness and turn subject into object), then not only might you start to let go of your present level or structure of consciousness (your Growing Up level), you will definitely start to let go of your presently identified-with major state of consciousness—thus, you will start to dis-identify with your structure-self and your state-self, opening yourself to the next-higher stage in both sequences.

(Now this is a complex issue, because the only thing that is “guaranteed” is that one’s state center of gravity will start to shift; whether one’s structure center of gravity also shifts depends on many, many factors, especially cultural and social. Remember, these structures are “hidden,” and so simply “looking within” won’t automatically make them object; they can—and most often will—simply remain hidden and unconscious. Introspecting, as we’ve seen, just doesn’t automatically make these hidden maps un-hidden and

obvious or object, just as no amount of meditation will show you the rules of grammar that you will still be following even if you reach Enlightenment. So keep in mind that the only thing that meditation “guarantees” is that your state center of gravity will begin to shift to higher and higher levels or state-stages of Waking Up; your structure or level of Growing Up might remain exactly where it was when you started. This is why, with Integral Meditation, we are intentionally bringing the structure-stages into awareness so as to definitely make them objects of awareness, which meditation alone will not necessarily do. In making subject object, you will then be making both structure-subject and state-subject into objects of awareness, thus transcending both and moving to the next higher stage in both developmental sequences, something that meditation alone will not do, and does not do. So please keep that in mind. . . .)

But, we were saying, one item that will almost certainly start happening as you take up a mindfulness-type of meditation, and you are presently identified with, say, the waking state, is that you will start to slowly become aware during dreaming states as well. Your Wakefulness will shift from just the waking into the dream state also. You will start to become conscious and aware of the subtle realm and all its secrets.

Now this might happen to a relatively small degree, and only occasionally; or it might happen big time, and you almost always start lucid dreaming. But the point is that, in either case, your center of gravity—your state center of gravity—is starting to expand (following that transcend-and-include increase of more wholeness, more inclusiveness, more embrace). You will start to notice that, for example, in your waking state, you identified with a lot of items, most of which were indeed gross or physical—you identified with your physical body, with your possessions (house, car, clothes), your relationships (especially their physically noticeable aspects—looks, sex appeal, height, etc.), your money and finances, your job, and so on. And yet in the dream state, virtually none of those are present, and yet you notice that you are still you. There is some sort of “I-ness” that remains the same “I” even though almost everything that it usually identifies with is gone—in the dream state, all the gross/physical items you usually identify with are either not present or exist in wildly different forms. But you are still you, or you still feel that you are you, even if you dream you are “someone else.” YOU are dreaming, the same YOU that will soon be awake eating breakfast.

As you start to notice this, and that awareness becomes stronger and

stronger, the stranger the whole thing will start to appear. You really do feel that you are the same you in the dream state as you are in the waking state, but nothing else is similar. Yet there is some sort of awareness, of Wakefulness, of consciousness, that essentially remains unchanged in you, whether you are “awake” or “sleeping”—in both cases, you are actually AWAKE. You are somehow the same awareness, the same you-ness, the same I AMness, the same consciousness that you are when awake—there is something so deep in you that it doesn’t matter whether typically “awake” or “asleep”—it is one and the same and unchanging. And this is a sense of self, this is a self-awareness, that is very, very different from the standard, waking-state, physical-body-identified ego. Something else is going on here. What is that?

What that is, is your getting closer and closer to your Real Self, your True Self, your Original Face, and a self that every major Great Tradition maintains is one with Spirit, with the “Supreme Identity.” As we will continue to see (and soon directly experience in person), this means your True Self is timeless and eternal, is Unborn and Undying; it doesn’t even enter the stream of time. A Zen koan says—and it means this absolutely literally—“Show me your Original Face \[your True Self\], the Face you had before your parents were born.” As I said, this is meant literally—you had this Real Self of yours before your parents were born, before the universe was born, before time itself—simply because it doesn’t enter the stream of time at all, but is a timeless Now, an eternal Present, awareness—and as your state center of gravity shifts closer and closer to the empty Witness (or True Self) and the nondual “unity” consciousness (the oneness of that True Self with Spirit itself and the entire universe), then the more that timeless eternity becomes obvious. (And remember, “eternal” or “timeless” does not mean everlasting time—it means a moment without time, a timeless Now or Present. As Wittgenstein wrote, “If we take eternity to mean not everlasting temporal duration but timelessness, then eternal life belongs to those who live in the Present.”3 And the True Self is plugged directly into this timeless Now, as we will soon see directly.)

You will be able to directly experience this yourself and get some real evidence, when we do the exercises that directly put you in touch with both of these highest states of consciousness. So until then—our very next section —please stick with me.

As your meditation practice continues to deepen, and you move from

subtle-state dreaming into the next-higher state-stage—that of the pure, dreamless, formless causal realm—that shift in your state center of gravity continues, and as you enter the deep dreamless state, a very tacit awareness will nonetheless still tend to be present, and you will realize in a very delicate fashion that, once again, even though virtually nothing is present in that state —it is a “dreamless, formless” state—YOU ARE STILL YOU. There is still a very, very subtle degree of awareness or profound Wakefulness or pure consciousness that is present—and you still feel exactly you, exactly the same I AMness that you were when there were objects present. (Individuals who achieve this state-stage show a distinctive EEG brain-wave pattern— deep delta waves, characteristic of deep dreamless sleep, but with added alpha waves, which are characteristic of the waking state, suggesting that both states are simultaneously present. That is, there is a Wakefulness in the dreamless state of these individuals.)

This formless, dreamless state drives home the sense of a Self that is indeed not dependent upon being identified with any objects whatsoever; that instead is radically Free, Liberated, Released, Emancipated—and I’m using a capital S in “Self” to start emphasizing this. This Self is very close indeed to that Original Face that you have had since before your parents were born, before the Big Bang, before time itself—and is a Self not bound to suffering, anxiety, fear, craving, lusting, or loathing, but rather is Open, Clear, Free, Transparent, Spacious—truly your Real Self, one with Spirit, one with the Kosmos, one with the All.

Now, the reason we are moving through a brief description of these first three states, and directly to exercises for experiencing the two highest states, is that in those highest states, this prior, radical, ultimate Supreme Identity or True Self is directly and immediately available and obvious. You will see that they are, indeed, ever-present—they are actually fully present in you right now, in this moment, and we only need to point to them for you to be able to recognize this and see them.

Of course, there is absolutely nothing wrong—and much right—about taking up a formal meditation practice and directly moving through all of these major state-stages fully. And there are literally hundreds of absolutely first-rate books available on this topic, in all of the major Traditions. Most towns and cities worldwide now have meditation centers and schools of practice of some kind. (But I must warn you that you will not find any school that is aware of both the stages of Growing Up and the stages of Waking Up,

so please be very, very careful about what you accept from teachings that you encounter.) But we are now going to move directly to the conclusion of this whole process, and do some exercises right now to realize both the True Self and Ultimate Unity Awareness—the end result of the whole process of Waking Up. (Most people already have some sense of waking, dreaming, and deep sleep; but very few have directly experienced and realized these two highest states, even though they are indeed ever-present; and since they do in fact embody the final culmination of this whole state sequence, we are going to move directly to them.) And if you’ve never been exposed to anything like this before, I think I can safely say, be prepared to be shocked.

Turiya: The Supreme Witness

Let’s start with the Witness, the fourth major state. (The word turiya is Sanskrit for “fourth,” and it is literally the fourth major state after gross, subtle, and causal; but again, the general notion is found worldwide, from “Christ-consciousness” to “Nous” in Plotinus to “Purusha” in Yoga philosophy.)

So sit back, relax your mind, and right now become aware of what you feel is your self. Just very simply, be aware of what you call your self. Then briefly describe it to yourself. You might think, “I am this many years old, I weigh this many pounds, I’m this tall, I went to this college, I have this degree, I work at this job, I’m in this relationship with a person whose name is such-and-such, I enjoy being on the computer, I like movies and all kinds of music, next week is my birthday,” and so on. (Go ahead and do this at least briefly. It’s important that you get a good, general, “objective” view and awareness of your general self as you see it and feel it right now. The following introduction to your True Self will work much better the more you do this preliminary, so take at least a few minutes and ponder this self of yours. . . .)

But then notice that this process actually involves two selves. One is the self that you are aware of as an object, the one that you just saw and described. Those descriptions were all objects that you could see, they are all things that are seen about yourself. But then there is the Self that is doing the seeing, the Self that is doing the describing, the actual Seer, the Observing Self, the Witness. This Self does the seeing, but cannot itself be seen—no more than an eye could see itself or a tongue could taste itself. It’s a pure

Subject or pure Seer, not an object or something seen. The 20th-century Zen master Zenkai Shibayama called this “Absolute Subjectivity.” Ramana Maharshi called it the “I-I”—the big “I” aware of the small “I.” If you try to find this Seer, and you end up seeing anything, that’s just more objects, not the real Subject, the real Seer, the real Self. The real Seer, the real Self, has an awareness that instead is much more like: “I see the mountain, but I am not the mountain. I have sensations, but I am not those sensations. I have feelings, but I am not those feelings. I have thoughts, but I am not those thoughts. I am not anything that can be seen; I am the pure Seer itself.”

As you rest in this pure Seer, this Witness, you won’t see any particular objects (whatever objects you see are fine); rather, all you will begin to notice is a sense of Freedom, a sense of Openness, a Space or Clearing—you are not identified with any objects at all; you are their Witness. As the Witness of them, you are Free of them. You have feelings, you are aware of feelings, but you are not those feelings—you are Free of them. You have thoughts, you are aware of thoughts, but you are not those thoughts—you are Free of them. All this time you have been identifying with some of these objects that you can actually see, and you have been mistaking them for a real self—but they’re not a real self, not a real subject; they’re objects, they are something seen, not the true Seer. That first self, which you just described to yourself, is actually what you are not—not really and truly and deeply. (It is, in fact, connected with one of the 7 or 8 hidden maps, the basic levels, of Growing Up that we have already discussed. In each case, they are the small, conventional, finite self and its hidden maps through which the Witness is looking at the world. As soon as you use mindfulness to make that subject an object, then the next higher level conventional self and its hidden map can emerge, and you will identity with that, and the Witness will then look through that map at the world, seeing and interpreting the world through the characteristics of that map. And so on until all subjects, all hidden maps, have been made objects, have been dis-identified with, at which point the only thing left is the pure Seer, the True Self, the Witness, Absolute Subjectivity, radical Openness and Emptiness and pure Freedom—and then the final step of its move into nondual unity consciousness, which we will discuss next.)

And that is why the meditative traditions the world over maintain that we are the victims of a huge case of mistaken identity—we have identified the Seer with something that can be seen. (Patanjali, the great Indian codifier of Yoga teachings, said in his Yoga Sutras that the ignorant, unenlightened ego

is due to “the identification of the Seer with the instruments of seeing.”)4

And whereas the Seer, the Witness, is infinite (and one with Spirit), the seen self, the little ego, is finite, partial, limited, fragmented, and terror-ridden. The Witness has no fear, it is the Witness of fear. The Witness is not the victim of life, it’s the Witness of life.
