people say that an individual is “too abstract” or “too heady,” they almost always mean an overemphasis on the logico-mathematical line—which is another proposed multiple intelligence with a logical, analytical manner that indeed can be rather dry and abstract), but what the “embodiment” enthusiasts should be celebrating is not the body instead of the head, but the unity and integration of both mind and body, intellect and heart, thoughts and feelings. Veering into feelings alone—which, again, in and by themselves cannot take the role of other—tends to veer a person into highly narcissistic modes of being and awareness, which did, indeed, mark the Baby Boomer “Me generation” that was first significantly motivated by the pluralistic “sensitive” stage. It is a mistake that we need not repeat.

Intra-personal intelligence. The prefix “intra” means “within, and so intra-personal intelligence is the capacity to cleanly and clearly introspect, to look within. In the words of the Gospels, “The kingdom of Heaven lies within,” but that won’t do you any good if you cannot look within in the first place. Everything from self-knowledge to wisdom depends upon being able to look clearly within. Again, this is not a capacity that simply comes with being human, at birth; it is the result of a long, difficult, complex path of growth and development, moving from egocentric introspection (not a real introspection, just amorphous feeling states) to ethnocentric introspection (being able to take the role of other, awareness can also begin to see into itself as well) to worldcentric introspection (the full-fledged emergence of the capacity to look within and clearly introspect; a major transformation). With this, the whole “interior space” opens up fully for the first time, and the emergence of the capacity to see “conditional worlds”—of “what if?” and “as if”—allows awareness to explore some of the nooks and crannies of this new interior world. But, as is the case with so many human capacities, none of this is given at the start.

Somatic intelligence (or kinesthetic intelligence). Also called “body intelligence,” this line is your capacity to read your body’s wisdom and be aware of various bodily states and conditions, as well as utilize your body’s many skills. An entire organic wisdom is embedded in your body, and being able to read that is a crucially important intelligence.

Moral intelligence. Knowing the morally right thing to do is moral intelligence. We treat others fairly if we feel some sort of identity or solidarity with them, and thus, as our identities grow wider and wider through the 6-to-8 levels of development, our moral response grows greater and

greater as well. As we just saw, a simple version of this is from egocentric (I care only for myself) to ethnocentric (I care for my group) to worldcentric (I care for all humans) to Kosmocentric (I care for all sentient beings).

“Morals” are different from “ethics.” Ethics involve the rules and regulations that any particular culture or group takes to be the way things should be done. It focuses on what is good according to that culture. Morals, on the other hand, focus not just on what is correct for a particular group, but what is correct according to universal principles—not what is good, but what is right. And ethics may, or may not, be very moral. Ethics or the “good” tends to be a validity claim for the Lower Left quadrant, a judging of what is taken as correct or just or appropriate for a given group, organization, or culture. Most professional organizations, for example, have a code of ethics, or rules governing that profession—medical ethics, legal ethics, political ethics (which are not always that moral, note). Even the Mafia has its own code of ethics—and it is not that moral (it’s a red/amber ethnocentric stage of moral development). The Mafia’s “code of silence” is one such ethical rule, which all members are expected to abide by (often on pain of death).

Morals tend to be a reflection of one’s given ethics, a meta-ethical judgment that determines whether the particular code of ethics is universally right or not. What’s good for the Mafia is not right for all humans. This is why morals are a judgment (of universal rightness); ethics are a validity claim (of appropriateness or goodness for a given group). Thus, ethics are confined to the Lower Left quadrant. A moral judgment, on the other hand, can be made in regard to phenomena from any quadrant (what is the right thing to do with the I or the we or the it, not just what is appropriate for the we). “Morals” is a multiple intelligence, because it is a universal developmental stream, going through 6-to-8 major levels, that is cross-culturally valid. “Ethics” is not a multiple intelligence or a universal developmental line, because it often varies from culture to culture, and at different times in a culture’s history; it is a mixture of some moral elements with lots of cultural preferences, tastes, idiosyncrasies, and historical variables, some of which may be fairly moral and some of which can be frankly immoral. There is such a thing as “ethical intelligence,” but it just refers to your capacity to read your culture’s backgrounds, contexts, and rules and roles, and what you end up seeing will be determined in large part by your own stage of moral development. “Ethics,” again, is confined to the Lower Left quadrant—it’s a reading of your culture’s contextual background (a validity claim of

appropriateness). “Morals” is a judgment not of “appropriateness” or “goodness” but of “rightness,” which can be made in relation to phenomena found, not just in the Lower Left, but in any quadrant—including, for example, “What is the morally right thing to do in relation to the Lower Right ecological system we find ourselves in?” or “What is the morally right thing to do with this lost stash of money that I found \[in the Upper Right\]?” or “What is the right thing to do with this idea that I’m claiming as my own \[in the Upper Left\] but that was discovered by somebody else?” or “What is the right thing to do with ethical laws whose meaning \[in the Lower Left\] I find actually immoral—perhaps I should engage in civil disobedience?”—and so on. People can engage in things like civil disobedience or nonviolent protest, showing that morals can indeed transcend or trump ethics (civil disobedience, of a Gandhi or a Rosa Parks, is the claim that certain ethical rules or laws in a particular culture are actually immoral from a higher, universal perspective, and should not be followed because doing so is immoral; the person committing civil disobedience is prepared to be judged by the culture as being “illegal,” but their point is that in this case “illegal” is not “immoral,” but just the opposite, it’s highly moral—hence the disobedience, often a tremendously virtuous, courageous act, following the individual’s “conscience”—that is, their moral intelligence).

Morals, as an intrinsic intelligence, goes with the “grain of the Kosmos”— namely, transcend-and-include—and its stages of growth reflect increasingly ever-wider (transcendent) circles of identification (inclusion). In other words, those sentient beings will be fully included in the moral awareness and moral action—egocentric to ethnocentric to worldcentric to Kosmocentric (a summary of the 6-to-8 major levels of development; and hence a summary applicable to every multiple intelligence).

Spiritual intelligence is how we think about Spirit, about ultimate concern, about what matters most, at each of the levels of spiritual Growing Up, in which the structures of consciousness in this intelligence move through their own version of the 6-to-8 major levels. But as we saw earlier, Growing Up is just one of two major but quite different types of spiritual engagement that humans have. The other is Waking Up, representing states of consciousness involving direct spiritual experience—in other words, the ways that we directly experience Spirit. But spiritual intelligence, as the term is most often used, refers to our intellectual or narrative understanding of spirituality, which was the first form of religion we discussed earlier, as first

studied by, for example, James Fowler (who, no surprise, found that it moves through 6 to 7 major levels—quite similar to the levels we have outlined in rainbow colors, but this time as they appear in the spiritual line).

Speaking of these two major forms of spiritual engagement, the problem with religion in the modern Western world is twofold: first, it has become fixated at the amber mythic-literal level of its spiritual Growing Up development, thus being around two millennia behind evolution (and Spirit-in-action) itself; and second, Western religion is almost completely out of touch with direct spiritual experience (in meditation or contemplation), and thus has no Waking Up to speak of.

Although Western spirituality historically had many schools of contemplative Waking Up development, the Church increasingly de-emphasized them, since they tended to run counter to established dogma, starting with the mystics’ almost universal experience of some version of the Supreme Identity, or the identity of soul and God—but only one person was ever acknowledged as having had that condition, and that was the Son of God himself. Claiming it for oneself was therefore pure blasphemy, and the Inquisition increasingly took upon itself the role of banishing any such notions from individuals’ minds, often through torture and mandatory death sentences. This, needless to say, was not an incentive to contemplative development, and the religious engagement of individuals increasingly focused on legalistic creeds, codes, and mythic-literal dogma of a particular stage of spiritual Growing Up, namely the mythic-literal. And so we ended up with the two major problems with religion in today’s Western world: no spiritual Waking Up, and rather low levels of spiritual Growing Up. Taken together, this is a cultural disaster of the first magnitude. I just can’t emphasize enough what a staggering nightmare this has been for Western civilization.

So what is the solution to this catastrophe? The recommendation is twofold, in the most general terms: (1) recognize all of the stages/levels of spiritual Growing Up, instituting a “conveyor belt” of transformation through those 6-to-8 levels by languaging and presenting the Tradition’s basic teachings and practices as they appear at each of those major structure-stages (a magic teaching and practice, a mythic teaching and practice, a rational teaching and practice, a pluralistic teaching and practice, and an integral teaching and practice; and (2), introduce (or re-introduce) the Tradition’s own schools on the practices of Waking Up, helping individuals move from a state

of sin, separation, finitude, contraction, and suffering, and through gross to subtle to causal to pure Witnessing, and finally to ultimate nondual Unity— their own deepest, highest, truest, most real, and most liberating Supreme Identity with Spirit and the entire manifest Kosmos, a Great, Glorious, Grand Liberation, and Emancipation.

Addressing both of those will move us from a traditional Enlightenment or Awakening—which tracks only states from gross through nondual—to a complete or full Enlightenment or Awakening—which is the highest state experienced by the highest structure to date. In other words, the highest state of Waking Up interpreted and experienced by the highest stage of spiritual intelligence (or spiritual Growing Up) produced yet by evolution. In today’s world, that means nondual unity consciousness interpreted by the Integral (on the edge of Super-Integral) level.

Willpower. Will is simply the power of the mind to push things; it is will that allows us to take a course of action and follow it directly, without losing track of our goal or veering off course. Will is required to put any human intention into action, and is thus a tremendously important intelligence. We’ll do some exercises in a moment to show you exactly what is involved with will (as we’ll do with the other multiple intelligences as well).

Self line. The self line of development concerns our relative, finite self as it grows into deeper, wider, higher, and more-encompassing forms. This is one of the most significant intelligences that humans possess, since, following the lead of consciousness, the human self goes from id to ego to God; from sub-human to human to superhuman; from subconscious to self-conscious to superconscious. Each of the 6-to-8 relative finite selves—with its various hidden maps—is what ever-present Witnessing or the True Self uses as something through which to look at, and interpret, the world (at each of those stages). Thus, the “self line” refers to the growth of this relative self.

Thus, the self line as studied by Western developmental psychologists (Loevinger, Kegan, Broughton, etc.) is a study of the finite structure-self and its development (all Western developmental studies focus on finite structures, self or otherwise). As a multiple intelligence, that variable is what the Growing Up self line will continue to represent (namely, the development of the finite structure center of gravity as it unfolds through the 6-to-8 structure-stages of the self-system’s own growth and development line). But in addition to that self line in Growing Up, we always have to remember to also include the self-system as its state center of gravity progresses through the 4–

5 major state-stages of Waking Up. Both of those together give us the dual center of gravity that all humans possess: for example, (amber, subtle), meaning an amber structure-self and a subtle state-self; or (green, causal) or (integral, nondual), and so on. Both of them together (self through structure-stages and self through state-stages)—or the dual center of gravity (one in Growing Up and one in Waking Up)—will track the overall development of the separate-self sense as it increasingly gives way to Supermind and Big Mind.

When all state-subjects have been made object, the result is Big Mind; when all structure-subjects have been made object, the result is Supermind. (Those are the two major axes of development, through states resulting in Big Mind and through structures resulting in Supermind.) The aim of Integral development is a Big Mind experienced by a Supermind—or the highest Waking Up state and highest Growing Up structure.

Now, unfortunately, you won’t find many books on this “dual-self” development (except mine on general Integral Theory and a few others focusing on Integral approaches—e.g., Ingersoll and Zeitler, Integral Psychotherapy), but you can easily track this topic yourself, to begin with, by learning about the various states and state-stages of consciousness development (see, e.g., my own forthcoming book, The Religion of Tomorrow, or the works of Daniel P. Brown and Dustin DiPerna, among others), and then learning to appraise your own state-self development through those major 4–5 states of unfolding. This, combined with your understanding of the 6-to-8 major structure-stages of development, will allow you to track this “dual center of gravity” of your own self—your general or overall (average) structure-stage of development and your general or overall (average) state-stage of development, as you continue to grow and evolve toward Big Mind (in states) and to Supermind (in structures). The dual center of gravity is simply where your proximate or central self is in both Growing Up and Waking Up.

So each of the “intelligences” that we have surveyed above are areas— developmental lines or streams—through which your True Self, your Supreme Identity, can shine. They are also some of the basic areas that your small, conventional, finite self will be identified with. That is, your small self —in addition to being at its own stage of development in structures and in

states—will be identified with a given level (or hidden map) in each of those overall lines. (One of the reasons the conventional, finite self-system is so important is that it is the major integrator and unifier of all the various manifest components and elements found in your being and awareness including all the developmental lines or multiple intelligences. As the medieval Scholastics put it, the self is that which gives unity to the mind.) The Integral aspect of mindfulness practice allows you to spot these lines, to awaken them, activate them, grow and develop them; and ultimately, if you keep at it, reach the highest, the most developed, the most whole, of all the levels in the major lines used in your life, thus increasing the reach and radiance of your own Self-Realization.

The idea, as always, is simply to become aware of each of these intelligences in you, as they are now arising and responding to the world around you (and we’ll do that specific practice in just a moment). These intelligences have a fair amount of independence, which means in many cases, you might find that you are quite highly developed in some lines; mediumly developed in other lines; and fairly poorly developed in yet others. (I always use the example of Nazi doctors: high cognitive intelligence, low moral intelligence.)

It’s very possible that, prior to reading the sections above, you were unaware of the many multiple intelligences that you have available—and that are nonetheless actively functioning in you right now—just as it is likely you were unaware of the 6-to-8 major developmental levels through which all of these lines are growing and evolving. Same with quadrants and states and types, most likely. But the whole point is that, once you begin learning about each of them, you can then use your own understanding—we call this using “self as instrument” (consciously using the faculties of the self to make judgments about the self)—to judge just where you are in each of these areas. Studies have shown that individuals, even general laypeople, are quite good at judging the degree of development—“higher” or “lower”—of various statements, ideas, and thoughts presented to them. And particularly when given some actual maps of the territory—and we have ourselves presented maps that include 6-to-8 major levels or structures, 8 major lines, 4–5 major states, and 4 major quadrants/views—your native capacity to use “self as instrument” will be greatly enhanced, and you will be able to form a fairly accurate assessment of just where you are in your overall growth and development in all of those areas. You’ll therefore be able not only to more

helpfully tailor any practices or exercises that might help your self-actualization, but also to notice which, of all these areas, you might need extra help with—and thus search out the appropriate sources of such help. But on the whole, with this overall process, self-understanding—and “world understanding”—increases monumentally.

One of the wonderful things about an “Integral Index” is using the overall Integral Framework to locate and place all phenomena now arising in the universe. It’s sort of like taking Big Data—which is just a ton of data with no obvious relationships to each other—and then actually arranging it all according to an ordered comprehensive Frame or Index, which is what AQAL allows you to do. Among other things, this not only helps identify the most utterly crucial and central areas of human potential, but it also shows how each and every one of them relates to all the others, and further, can be activated, accelerated, grown, and evolved. This is truly an amazing breakthrough in human history; and, despite all the world’s truly wicked problems, it’s an astonishing time to be alive, with these sorts of new and exciting discoveries pouring over us.

So, back to topic: in addition to taking up specific exercises for growing and enhancing each of those developmental lines, you can bring mindfulness to bear on them as well, thus accelerating the movement through each of those line’s various levels (that is, moving through the 6-to-8 major levels of development as they appear in each of these lines of development). Let’s do a quick experiential run-through of this “mindfulness of lines” just to see what’s involved, very simply.

Mindfulness of Lines

I’m going to mention the name of a line, give a few major characteristics of it, and I’d like you to focus on whatever thoughts or feelings come up when I do. Let those thoughts and feelings arise, and then hold them in mind, and look at them from all directions—really videotape them, and then especially ask: where in the body is this located, what does it look like, and what does it feel like? When you practice this on your own, you can spend more time on each line than we will be doing now (and you can ask the full battery of mindfulness questions); but for now, just a quick run-through.

Cognitive intelligence. Let’s start with being aware of awareness. Notice awareness itself. Just take a few minutes to rest in awareness, then see your

awareness itself as an object (to the extent that you can), and ask your questions: what does it look like, what does it feel like, where is it located? See awareness as an object of awareness. (This might lead you back to the Witness—if so, fine, go ahead and let that happen.)

Emotional intelligence. Simply get a sense of what you are feeling right now. What emotions are arising (excitement, joy, confusion, boredom, happiness, anxiety, sadness, delight, exhilaration)? Take a few moments and really see and feel your feelings as objects. If there are others around you, take their role, put yourself in their shoes, and practice mindfulness on what you imagine they are perceiving and feeling. Be mindful not only of your 1st-person perspective, but also of your 2nd-person perspective. Make both of them object. Really let your feelings run in the overall Field of Awareness— but do not get taken away by them!

Intrapersonal intelligence. Very simply, right now, just look within. Introspect. Just look at what you’re thinking, feeling, hoping, wishing, wanting, needing, desiring. Be aware of your interiors, see your interiors as object and hold that awareness—what do they look like, feel like, where is each located? Notice this whole overall feel of “looking within”—what is that?

Somatic/bodily intelligence. Directly and immediately feel your body. Just your overall body. It’s often implicitly part of your subjective self; now make it an object. Look at it, feel it, see it. Hold it in your total Field of Awareness. Look at it, don’t feel through it.

As you do this, you might increasingly slip into a Witnessing position, realizing that because you can see the body as an object, your True Self “has a body but is not this body.” And indeed it is not. The True Self, the pure Witness, sees the body arising as an object in Awareness just like any other object—that mountain, those clouds, that building, those trees, this body . . . all neti, neti. I-I am not this, not that; I-I am Free of all of that. When the Witness further dissolves into nondual Suchness or One Taste, you will then find an identity with the body—AND with every other object arising, including that mountain, those clouds, that building, those trees, and, yes, this body. But then they are all simply elements in the Total Painting of All That Is, and you are ALL of that, totally one with the entire Painting and all of its elements. Thus, to your complete Freedom is added a total Fullness. This can happen with mindfulness applied to any of the multiple intelligences, but the body particularly lends itself to this, as it is the major source of subjective

identity, and thus a crucial switch point from our partial identities with the isolated organism to our Total Identity with All That Is.

Moral intelligence. Remember the last time you were faced with a moral dilemma—what is the right thing to do? Picture the wrong thing to do, and then picture the right thing to do—and then hold that feeling of rightness in your awareness. See it as an object. Just what does it look like, what does it feel like, this “doing the right thing”? Why is that important? What do you feel like when you imagine doing the wrong thing? What’s wrong about being wrong? What thoughts and feelings does that generate? (Again, the aim is not then to do something with this or about this, but simply to see it, to be aware of it, to hold it as an object in your total Field of Awareness. “Nothing happens next. . . .”) Simply videotape that profound feeling of being moral, being right.

Spiritual intelligence. Think very carefully about what it is that is of ultimate concern to you. What is the single most important thing in the whole world to you. You might think of several—your mate, your kids, your job, your money, your friends, your reputation, your health—but force yourself to choose just one. Then hold that item in mind, and pay attention to the feeling of wanting it (or simply cherishing it). Notice that ultimate wanting—see it as an object: what does it feel like, look like, where is it located? What does it mean to have an ultimate concern? What does “ultimate” even mean?

Again, and this is worth repeating—don’t expect a particular answer, or any answer, to come up—and don’t imagine you are supposed to do anything about whatever comes up, or take a specific action in response—your only task is to simply be aware of whatever arises when you address these issues —just take whatever comes up, including a possible sense of just blankness, and look directly at it—videotape it—and that’s it, that’s all that is required. Seeing whatever comes up as an object is your only task, even if what comes up is just a blank unfocused stare. . . .

Willpower. Look at the seconds sweep hand of your watch (or seconds on a digital readout), and hold your mind on that item as it clicks along for 15 seconds. All the while feel the power of your mind holding on to those seconds readouts as they occur—notice your capacity to hold attention on that single area without losing track. Feel that mental strength. Look at it closely as an object—what it feels like, what it looks like, this thing called will.

Noticing this capacity to focus, along with its very positive qualities, is

also what moves Awareness to attention—and thus is the source of Primordial Avoidance as well. So making that capacity to focus an object is also helping to make Primordial Avoidance an object as well, thus releasing background Awareness to return to its full and relaxed state reflecting the Total Painting of All That Is, without oppressively focusing on one element to the exclusion of others—or avoiding one element rather than others. Again, don’t do this mindfulness session with that as a goal or aim; just making it object is the aim. But at various points in the exercise, you might find yourself slipping into that fundamental Fullness—which of course is just fine!

Self line. And finally, your own self-contraction. Simply feel, right now, that tiny interior tension that you associate with your separate-self sense. This sensation will be a combination of the major state you are in and the structure that is interpreting and experiencing it. (As you get better and better at Integral Mindfulness, you will be able more clearly to distinguish these. The state-self will involve a present, obvious, 1st-person, immediate experience— involving gross objects, or subtle objects, or causal objects, or Witnessing moving into a pure “I-I” experience or even further into a headless unity experience. And you will see and interpret your world and experience with your major structure-stage—thus, for example, if you are predominantly at orange achievement, hold on to the idea or image of your goal of achieving, succeeding, accomplishing—and that thought-value is part of your structure-self; and unlike the state, the structure itself is not directly obvious, unless you use a developmental map to highlight it (as we are doing now).

Yet the self-sense is not a thing but a process, a process of subtly contracting your awareness. Be aware of that contraction, that feeling of self; hold that subtle tension in your mind, videotape it with awareness, see it as an object. (You can very likely feel that tiny tension, that self-contraction, right now, while “under” it or “beyond” it is the ever-present Witness or headless unity consciousness—and by making the self-contraction object, you will more likely fall into one of these much higher states!)

Other developmental lines. After having looked briefly at those 8 major streams, we might ask: How many other developmental lines or multiple intelligences are there? This is a widely debated topic in developmental studies. There have been dozens and dozens of items introduced that are loosely claimed to be “multiple intelligences,” some of them feeble in the extreme. But we might at the least mention that—of those that have a

modicum of reasonableness or evidence for their existence—in addition to the 8 above, there have also been suggested: logico-mathematical intelligence, musical intelligence, aesthetic, relationship, linguistic, perspectives, psychosexual, values, defenses, interpersonal development, needs, worldviews, naturalistic, spacetime, existential, and gender, among others. The 8 that I have included are ones that cover many of the most central bases of what multiple intelligences are all about, and taking those into account is the least that we need to do in order to cover this dimension of our own being. But neither do we really need to cover much more of them for a general introductory awareness of this area of our own core. Of course, we can include as many intelligences as we wish; and as for personal growth and development, we can take up any number of practices to enhance the qualities —in both vertical altitude and horizontal aptitude—of any of these intelligences that we wish. The point is to get a fairly clear and distinct awareness of at least these 8 different capacities as a clear object of awareness, thus continually turning subject into object and opening ourselves to ever-higher, ever-wider, evermore-inclusive capacities in our very own being. And as we familiarize ourselves with these 8 intelligences, a very important fact starts to become more and more obvious: we can be smart in many different ways—so don’t shortchange yourself!

It’s often said that you should find something that you love, and that you have some natural talent in, and then pursue that wholeheartedly, and your dreams will almost automatically come true. Well, alas, it might not be quite that simple. But one thing is for sure—if you don’t know about all of the intelligences that you actually have access to, you might very likely miss the talent that you are actually and truly gifted in. So keep your eyes open with these intelligences! And—as with the rest of the Integral quest—be sure to look for those areas that have largely between ignored, denied, overlooked, or dismissed, because those might indeed be the very home where your genius resides. As usual, the Integral Framework—by moving away from the “one size fits all”—opens you to an enormous number of areas and dimensions and capacities and skills whose existence you might not have even suspected— and yet they hold the hidden treasure of your own most brightly shining talents!

The Integral Psychograph

If we want to get a sense of how of we are doing in our “levels and lines” (i.e., how well developed—the level—we are at each of our multiple intelligences—the lines), we can draw these together in what is called an “Integral psychograph”—an actual graph showing all of these parameters. So going up the vertical axis are the major levels of development we have discussed (from 1st tier to 2nd tier to 3rd tier—usually represented by their colors, but any indicator of vertical growth—names, numbers—will do). And across the horizontal line or axis, we list the particular developmental line(s) that we are tracking; each line is drawn as high, or as “far up,” as that line has developed to so far (up to red, or to amber, or orange, or green, etc., according to the vertical axis colors or other growth indicator). Then, at a glance, you can tell just how well you are doing in each major multiple intelligence—maybe fairly poorly (e.g., up to red); maybe fairly well (e.g., up to orange); maybe exceptionally well (e.g., up to turquoise)—and you can see this for each of the lines that you have entered on the psychograph.

The Integral psychograph can also be used to show the relation between different major developmental models. We mentioned that often a particular model focuses on just one multiple intelligence; sometimes it focuses on a few; sometimes a “package” of several. But one of the major reasons that these models often vary quite a bit from each other is that they are focusing on different multiple lines. This doesn’t make them less useful, let alone wrong, but is simply an indication of the rich variety of interwoven lines that make up the human being.

But however the Integral psychograph is used, the point is that the levels of development themselves don’t exist on their own anywhere; they are levels of development, for sure, but always levels of development in specific lines. The general altitude levels themselves show up in each line with both (1) the generic characteristics of the altitude as seen at each major level, and (2) the actual forms/contents of a level as expressed in and through the stages in a particular line. So, as we earlier noted, the amber altitude shows up in cognitive-line development as the concrete-operational thinking stage; in moral-line development, it shows up as the “law and order” stage; in self-line development, it shows up as the “conformist” self-stage; and so on. Different lines, same levels (or different streams, same waves). And again, the levels/waves do not exist anywhere on their own, but always manifest as stages in the specific lines. What all the levels have in common is the general altitude or degree of development itself; where they differ is in all the actual

details and specific characteristics that are provided by each of the lines in their own way.

Each of the pioneering developmental models in the West tended to focus on a specific multiple intelligence, and studied the general 6-to-8 developmental levels/stages as they showed up in that line. Thus, Jean Piaget focused on cognitive intelligence; Lawrence Kohlberg on moral intelligence; Jane Loevinger on ego intelligence; Clare Graves on values intelligence; Abigail Housen on aesthetic intelligence, and so on. Some models studied items that, as a distinct function of the psyche, tended to be a specific mixture of several intelligences (Kegan’s “orders of consciousness,” for example, includes cognitive, perspective, self, and worldview lines). But many of them did indeed manage to focus fairly clearly on one specific developmental line or multiple intelligence (Maslow’s needs, Fowler’s spiritual intelligence or “stages of faith,” Graves’s values intelligence, Kohlberg’s moral intelligence, and so on).

But whether we are looking at a developmental “package” that is essentially one major developmental line or a mixture of several developmental lines, we can create an Integral psychograph showing the relation of these various developing line-packages compared with each other, and based on the major common levels of vertical altitude development that all of the lines are traveling through (again: different lines, same levels).

The more developmental lines we include on any given psychograph—out of the 8 or more major ones, including well-known line-packages such as Kegan’s—the more complete a picture we have of the overall developmental status of the individual whose psychograph it is. We’ll return to creating an individual psychograph (such as you might do for yourself or someone you know) in just a moment.

LEVEL OF CONSCIOUSNESS

Figure 4.1. An Integral psychograph: some major developmental lines through the major (rainbow) levels. (To view this figure in a larger format,

visit www.shambhala.com/integralmeditation.)

In the meantime, figures 4.1 and 4.2 show two generalized psychographs— in these cases, focusing on major, well-known developmental models, showing how all of them actually fit together. The generic, vertical, common, major levels of development (or “levels of consciousness”)—with their rainbow colors (including the standard 6-to-8 levels given by their colors in the vertical column of 1st and 2nd tier, combined with the tentative 4 levels of 3rd tier and their colors, giving 12 Growing Up levels overall)—all of which are shown running up the vertical axis; and then the various lines of development or particular line-packages of various theoretical models (Graves, Kegan, “SD” for Spiral Dynamics, etc.) are shown across the horizontal dimension or axis, placed next to one another so that rapid comparisons can be made between their various stages (which is how each of the major developmental levels appears in that particular line at each altitude, or how the levels actually appear in stages of a particular line). Figure 4.2 also shows, at the end of the graph, a miniature reproduction of the meditation “circles” of the major states of consciousness (which we first saw in fig. 2.1), as a reminder that any of those states can be experienced by anyone at virtually any of those structure/levels.

You may recall that I referred to the 100 different developmental models that I included in charts in my book Integral Psychology. Well, every one of those could be included on a psychograph of this type, showing how all of them compare and contrast with each other, using the important variable of “altitude,” introduced by Integral Theory, to tie all of these various lines together via a universal spectrum of altitude development—different lines, same levels, no matter how different the surface features in each of the lines look at each altitude stage (both generic altitude levels and specific developmental lines occur together in every intelligence).

Creating an individual psychograph. Each individual can create their own Integral psychograph (using, as we noted, “self as instrument”). The material that you have already learned about each of the generic 6-to-8 vertical developmental levels in themselves (which is where literally 99% of development occurs at this time in evolution) is enough for you to intuitively guess (with a fair amount of accuracy) what general vertical stage you have developed up to in each specific multiple intelligence or particular developmental line. So you might reason, if you were a woman in senior year at college: “Cognitively I’m pretty bright (I keep hearing, ‘Especially for a girl,’ which is irritating); but perhaps even turquoise in that line. Emotionally, I’m a bit weak for my gender, perhaps amber. Morally, I’m strong and tend toward egalitarianism, or green. Somatically or kinesthetically, I’m very weak; it wouldn’t surprise me if I were magenta or red. I don’t know what the problem is there, but I’ve just never felt fully at home in this body. Intra-personal, very strong, green or teal. Will, also strong; perhaps orange or green (I get in trouble with this, but if I were a man, I would be congratulated on it!). Self line, pretty good, at least green. Musically, almost nil, zero; infrared. Logico-mathematical, not too bad, though it’s not my favorite, perhaps amber or orange. Linguistically, very strong, at least teal, maybe turquoise (I’ve been writing poetry since early teens, and it always gets strong compliments). Overall average level (or structure center of gravity) is therefore around orange to green (which also fits with the self-line stage, an important consideration for ‘center of gravity’).”

LEVEL OF CONSCIOUSNESS

Figure 4.2. An Integral psychograph: some more developmental lines through the major (rainbow) levels. (To view this figure in a larger format, visit

www.shambhala.com/integralmeditation.)

An Integral psychograph lets you see, in one big glance, just how you are doing in each of your developmental lines or multiple intelligences. Don’t be shy in considering something a multiple intelligence; as we just saw, there are many different definitions of exactly what a multiple intelligence is and what list of intelligences we actually do have; some people postulate an intelligence for almost any skill humans can undertake (including “architectural intelligence,” “dentistry intelligence,” “gardening intelligence,” etc.—you can go overboard here, so apply at least some reasonable restraint). But a psychograph is a wonderful way to track your own growth, development, and evolution through the various structures of consciousness that evolution has blessed us with.

But note, it is not necessary to have every single intelligence topping off at 2nd-tier turquoise or Integral (on the cataclysmic edge of the leap to 3rd tier). Items that are worth keeping an eye on include things like, given your chosen profession, are you well developed in all the intelligences that have been demonstrated to help create success in that profession? It has been suggested that business leaders, for example, are more successful the more developed they are in cognitive intelligence (particularly in its higher, 2nd-tier levels,

known as systemic or vision-logic); emotional intelligence; interpersonal intelligence;2 and finally (but increasingly) spiritual intelligence: for example, John Mackey, co-founder of Whole Foods, and Raj Sisodia, co-authors of Conscious Capitalism, use all 4 quadrants for their defining tenets, plus an all-level spectrum based in part on an Integral model, and include several developmental lines, such as cognitive intelligence, emotional intelligence, systemic intelligence, and, yes, spiritual intelligence. If you are a business leader, and serious about succeeding in the widest and deepest possible ways, then you will want to get serious about checking in yourself each of those intelligences that I just mentioned, noticing which are strong and which are weak, and then taking courses or other actions to polish your skills (aptitude and altitude) in each intelligence that truly needs it.

The point is that, with more Integral maps—which inherently include these areas marked out on the overall territory in which business functions—these types of decisions can be easily spotted and implemented. And again, you don’t have to excel in all lines or intelligences. Business leaders, although perfectly free to work on these intelligences if they wish, usually do not need to excel, for example, in musical intelligence, spatial intelligence, linguistic intelligence, or kinesthetic intelligence (although a specific business would want to include other lines if it focuses on products in those areas). The point is, you can be fairly described as “Integral” if your consciousness/cognitive lines, and your self-related lines, all spend a majority of their time in teal Holistic or turquoise Integral; and if you are at least fully cognizant of the various lines, and are significantly developed at least in those lines that serve the primary areas of your actual life (work, relationships, parenting, etc.).

But this is an exciting time if you are genuinely interested in the growth and development of your own deepest, widest, highest gifts and potentials and talents. Never have we known so much about the various potentials available to humans: ways to accelerate and fulfill our growth, what hampers or derails this growth, and ways to repair and reboot those potentials as needed. And as an added—and rather extraordinary—bonus, by doing so, not only do we increase our excellence in each of these potentials (including them in ever-higher identities), we also come closer and closer to the Ground and Goal and Source of them all—by transcending them into our very own True Self, Real Seer, and ultimate Suchness. The paths of Growing Up and Cleaning Up and Showing Up have all come together with the radical paths of Waking Up, so that our progress in expanding our relative, finite capacities

and gifts is also advancing us toward our realization of our ever-present True Self and ultimate nondual Suchness.

The potential for this combined benefit has literally never existed at any previous time in our entire history. But if you were born in the ’50s or ’60s or later, you are among the first humans in all of history to be able to awaken all 5 of the most fundamental dimensions of a human “being-in-the-world” (quadrants, levels, lines, states, and types). This is unbelievable, astonishing, outrageous, gorgeously glorious—and thank our lucky stars indeed (which are simply the twinkling eyes of our own Original Face, smiling back at us). . . .

The AQAL Matrix

Now to finish up with our discussion of the Integral Mindfulness of lines. We left off with a mindfulness of the self line, particularly aiming to make the little finite subject or self an object of Awareness. While you are doing that, while you are being aware of the self-contraction, bring this lines-awareness section to an end by being mindful of that which is being aware of the self-contraction. That is, be aware of the Witness of your “I” or your “me.” Remember that the Witness is what Ramana Maharshi called the “I-I” because it is the pure, seeing big “I” looking at the object self or small “I”— the pure Seer, the Real Self, the True Subject, and not the little finite ego subject that in fact can be seen as an object, and thus isn’t even a real subject or real self in the first place (which is why virtually every Great Tradition refers to it as “illusory,” “fallen,” “dualistic,” or contaminated with the “original sin” of separation). That Witness, that “I-I,” which was just aware of each of your lines or multiple intelligences, and was (in chapter 1) aware of each level of your 6-to-8 hidden maps, and was (in chapter 3) aware of each of your quadrants in relationships, is indeed your Original Face, your True Seer, your Real Self, and it is in actuality directly one with Spirit, one with pure Consciousness or pure infinite Awareness, which is one and the same in all sentient beings—as Erwin Schrödinger, the founder of modern quantum mechanics, said, “Consciousness is a singular of which the plural is unknown.”3 This is your Big Mind, your real Self, the pure Observing Self, the true Witness. Rest briefly in that pure Witnessing awareness—that pure and simple feeling of I AMness, right here, right now—and remember, it’s not something that can itself be seen—if you see anything, that’s just another

object. Rather, the Witness is the vast, pure, open, empty clearing or space in which all objects and subjects are arising right now, moment to moment to moment—the clearing these words are arising in, the clearing the clouds are arising in, the clearing the buildings are arising in, the clearing this room is arising in. That clearing is infinite Spaciousness, pure open Emptiness, your one and only True Self and Real Condition. Rest in that vast, open, empty, clear, still, silent Spaciousness, your timeless and eternal and real Being.

Now, that Witness, although itself without qualities or characteristics of any type—for it is radically unlimited, unborn, undying, unqualifiable—that Witness looks at the world through whatever Form is present to it. That means the Witness will look at the world through whatever level the conventional self is at (and through whatever the hidden maps are that it will use at whichever of the 6-to-8 major levels the self is presently at), AND the Witness will also look through the particular line that is now active, and it looks through whatever state is now arising, and it looks through whatever quadrant is being active, and through whatever type is being engaged (“type,” as we will especially see shortly, is whatever specific type in any typology that is being activated in the self-system at any given moment—the point is, the Witness will look through that type as well).

This is what Patanjali meant when he spoke of the false identification of the Seer with the instruments of seeing. That is, we identify our Real Self, our True Seer, the pure Witness, with whatever quadrant, level, line, state, or type is present (the sum total of these elements are called “AQAL,” short for “all quadrants, all levels, all lines, all states, all types”).

Integral Mindfulness allows us to see each of these elements and be aware of their existence, be conscious of them, often for the first time, thus releasing them from being objects that we were identified with as our little subject or self (that is, things we were ultimately identifying our True Self with), and instead turning them into mere objects of awareness, seeing these subjects as objects, thus dis-identifying with those objects and resting as our true, timeless, unborn, and undying Real Self.

In other words, this allows us to escape from any particular identification with the AQAL Matrix. When we are identified with various aspects of the AQAL Matrix—and we don’t know it—then we reduce our infinite and eternal Real Self and Suchness to a mere finite, temporal, spatial object, thus opening it to fragmentation, brokenness, torment, and torture, and missing our real and true Identity—our Supreme Identity. When thus exclusively

identified with components of the AQAL Matrix, that AQAL Framework becomes the floor plan of the prison in which we are enslaved, a map of our case of mistaken identity, a direct pointer to the items causing our pain and suffering in the manifest world.

On the other hand, when we see all of the various dimensions of the AQAL Matrix, we become conscious of them, we turn those subjective identities into objects of awareness—objects of our True Self, the pure Witness, genuine Awareness. Thus, just like Neo in The Matrix, when we are in the Matrix and don’t know it, it becomes our prison, giving us a reality that is actually illusory (although it seems very real). But when we escape the Matrix, when we see it from the outside, as an object of awareness, an object of mindfulness, then we become radically Free of it. We achieve the Great Liberation, an Enlightenment or Awakening to who and what we really are. From there, it’s a short step to the union of the pure Seer with everything seen, and that includes a union with the entire AQAL Matrix. Identified with no particular point or place in the Matrix, we are free to become one with all of it—to recognize all of our quadrants, levels, lines, states, and types as pure manifestations of Spirit, as Spirit.

And if our small, conventional self is identified with any of the AQAL Matrix—and of necessity, it will be—it will also be, following the Integral super-map, open to being one with the highest, most whole, most developed and evolved elements to date anywhere in the overall Matrix. Our headless, imageless, nondual unity of Emptiness and Form will be a unity with the Fullest Form possible (e.g., the Integral or Super-Integral level, and aware of all 4 quadrants, and all 8 lines, from the very highest nondual state)—in other words, the greatest amount of Form available, from the highest level or dimension that transcends and includes all the others, giving a Full Form indeed, not a fractured, limited, partial, broken, and tormented Form, but the Fullest Form as ornaments of pure Spirit itself, our ultimate and genuine nondual Suchness or Thusness, our True and Original Face, the core of our own Great Perfection.

As for each of those specific areas of life—health, diet, and fitness; career and money; family and parenting—each of those, in addition to being areas where the mindfulness portion of Integral Mindfulness can introduce you to your own True Self and your ultimate nondual Supreme Identity, can also be areas where, in the conventional, finite, manifest world (which is included in the Integral portion of Integral Mindfulness), you can grow, develop, polish,

and perfect each of the major dimensions in the AQAL Matrix, through which your conventional, finite self is operating, so that when your Real Self identifies with the manifest realm, it really will be the most developed and evolved and fullest and most Whole manifest realm possible—and it will create the most adequate, the most competent, the most Whole, and the utterly Fullest conventional self possible for one’s True Self and Suchness to manifest, express, and communicate itself, thus dramatically increasing one’s skillful means across the board.

Each of those areas—fitness, career, family, and so on—has a facet in each of the 4 quadrants (and needs to be addressed from all 4); each of them is being accessed from a particular level of development with its hidden map (although those levels can indeed be grown and developed and fully opened up); each of them calls upon a given selection of multiple intelligences (which also can be grown and developed and fully opened up); and each of them can become the object of state mindfulness meditation (i.e., awareness or mindfulness of the state it is in), leading ultimately to one’s True Self and one’s nondual Suchness or Thusness.

Again, this is the first time, essentially in all of history, that both Growing Up through developmental levels and Waking Up through developmental states (along with Showing Up in all 4 quadrants, Cleaning Up in shadow elements, and Opening Up in various multiple intelligences) have all been brought together, practiced together, and realized together, producing a truly super-human Realization in one’s very own being.

But, as exciting as this prospect is, it’s not necessary to rush into things. Much of this information is likely to be quite new to readers of this book. However, these factors are fully operating, right now, and they are profoundly governing how we interpret, see, and experience our world. We don’t have any choice about that—those areas are there, smashing into us come what may, like it or not, want it or not, believe it or not, know it or not. Our choice, rather, is whether we want to be aware of these various factors, or whether we’d just as soon continue to be blindsided by them.

If we choose the consciousness route, if we choose to be aware of these factors, it’s important to take it nice and easy, slow and steady, gently if firmly. It’s very easy to get a “metaphysical hernia” trying to lift too much of this material all at once. Rather, take a specific topic or issue—say, the 6-to-8 structures/levels, or the 4 or 5 major states/realms, or perhaps a bit of shadow work, or focusing on the quadrants—and work on it easily and loosely,

starting with 15–30 minutes a day, and slowly expanding from there. Don’t try and include a practice that crams all of these factors into place all at once. Some of them will clearly be more important in your life than others, and you can pick them up in an order that fits your interests, capacities, and desires. What’s important is that you’re now fully aware that these various territories do indeed exist; and, just as important, you have some good introductory maps of all of those territories (from 6-to-8 levels, to 8 lines, to 4 or 5 states, to 4 quadrants—and so on). Simply work a few of the most significant aspects of any of those territories into your life slowly and easily. As they become a comfortable habit, introduce another one or two, and so on until you are addressing as many of these profound territories as you wish in your own life and being and awareness.

If these areas continue to speak to you, you will likely become familiar with the various knowledge communities (or collections of specialists) dealing with each of these areas, as well as the international Integral community itself. If I may speak for all of these people, let me say that we gladly welcome you into this extraordinary gathering and miraculous adventure.

A Brief Glimpse at Types

I have saved “types” for the last AQAL element to introduce, simply because it is such a wide-open area. The idea of any decent model of reality is to use the fewest number of elements to explain the most amount of reality. That is what the 5 elements of AQAL Integral Theory do (namely, quadrants/views, levels/waves, lines/streams, states/realms—and types). The generic “types” is simply meant to hold a space for any number of typologies that might apply to a specific area that is being studied. A “type” does not tend to change from level to level, state to state, or line to line. Thus, the “masculine/feminine” spectrum—if you are feminine, you are feminine at every major level of development, and in every major state, and every major line. The “feminine” itself might grow and develop (and it does), but it is still the same basic “femininity” that is being tracked. Every quadrant has hundreds—indeed, thousands—of different typologies, each applying to a specific area or facet of existence.

“Types” is thus included as a generic element in the AQAL Framework to indicate that if you are approaching a particular topic in an Integral fashion,

you might want to include one or two (or even more) typologies dealing with that topic to fully flesh it out. Thus, if you are dealing with “personality” in the Upper Left, you might want to include typologies such as the Myers-Briggs Type Indicator, the Enneagram, and/or masculine/feminine (which I called “spectrum,” referring not to gender identity but to qualities that have been identified as masculine or feminine in developmental research, including but not limited to Gilligan’s masculine/feminine values). The Enneagram is a good example of the increased focus that a good typology can bring to the topic. According to that system, the human personality comes in 9 basic types. The names alone give an indication of how different these types are from each other: (1) perfectionist, (2) giver, (3) performer, (4) romantic, (5) observer, (6) questioner, (7) epicure, (8) protector, (9) mediator. And because those are types, then if a person is fundamentally, say, type 5, then they will be a type 5 at magenta, red, amber, orange, green, and so on. The different types are rarely as determining as, say, different levels or states, but they can have a profound influence; and especially if one is entering a particular area involving a great deal of detail, including a typology or two can definitely help. Individuals also tend to find that a good typology can really increase their own self-understanding. Most people who study the Enneagram, for example, find it to be right on the money not only about their own traits but also about the traits of other people they know. It significantly brings new and useful information to the table.

Now, I’m not going to say much more about typologies at this time. I simply wanted to point out their existence, and alert you to the fact that many very good and very effective typologies are available, and often including one or more of them is genuinely helpful in providing an even more wholistic and integrated view of a topic. If you pursue Integral Studies, you will find many typologies used across a broad spectrum of areas.

Okay, given all of the areas that we have investigated in the previous pages, what actual next steps could or should be taken? How shall we finally look at all this material—what difference does it actually make? If I’m already on a path of Waking Up, how can I effectively introduce, for example, stages of Growing Up (or any other element from the AQAL Framework)? What’s the real point of all of this? Does it really matter? What exactly am I supposed to take away from all this? What difference is it really going to make in my life?

Christina Hoff Sommers, “The Republic of Feelings,” September 1, 2000, http://www.aei.org/publication/the-republic-of-feelings/, accessed July 23, 2015. 2. Some would include moral intelligence, although this is a controversial inclusion, because the fact is that, the way business is arranged in today’s world, some actions that will benefit a business are less than moral, and successful business leaders are often willing to step over that line; a recent poll—carried in Harvard Business Review and reported in Time magazine, July 21, 2014—showed that 78% of chief executives would act immorally if it helped them meet Wall Street–defined goals; but this is an arena of intense recent debate, and any decent-minded person would have to start demanding genuinely moral actions from all business leaders—which, of course, requires that they actually have a moral intelligence up and running and at a fairly high altitude in the first place. 3. Erwin Schrödinger, “What Is Life?” (1943), in What Is Life? With Mind and Matter and Autobiographical Sketches (Cambridge: Cambridge University Press, 1992), p. 89.

The Total Painting of All That Is

Ultimately, what we are looking for is to have the Total Painting of All That Is really and truly be both “Total” and “All”—and if one thing has become clear in everything we have seen thus far, it is that numerous aspects of this Total Painting are hidden, are unaware, are whacking into us daily with us having no idea whatsoever about their nature or even existence. It’s simply not the case that a human being is born with an insured access to all the things that he or she deeply and truly needs to navigate this world (not to mention any “other world” we might consider). And given the very nature of evolution itself, with each new transcend-and-include movement, the “transcend” part brings a little bit of something new into existence—the Total Painting just got a bit bigger—and that is apparently unending. Which means there are truths that we not only cannot know now, but that don’t even exist now or haven’t yet emerged (and will only do so at some point in the future —contributing to every future’s realization of just how uninformed and ignorant all past humans actually were, including us).

But, as I said, in the meantime, as we continue to make the future we are given, let us at least “transcend and include” all past truths worth including, and for that we have to be aware of them in the first place. And we have seen that much of reality is actually not obvious, not given, not apparent, not running around with bells and whistles trying to get our attention; yet it is tucked into the most obvious corners of Kosmos, but in ways that just are not apparent, until approached with specific means or methods, at which point they jump out—or are co-created—with all the obviousness and clarity imaginable, making us think, “Why didn’t I see that before?”

The AQAL Framework is a list of items hidden from humanity in plain sight. Each of these areas has a small community of knowledge-holders who are generally in remarkable, broad agreement as to the nature of this “hidden” area—but whose work is virtually unknown outside of their specific small community. The AQAL Framework includes 5 of these areas (quadrants, levels, lines, states, and types) that are some of the absolutely most important areas in all of human life, each intensely studied by small communities

around the world, and it puts all of them together into a simple but comprehensive Framework that shows how they all are related, and allows us to start unpacking these areas with much greater awareness and interest, applying them in ways that make a stunning difference to human life and all its areas in general.

And this makes a difference not only in the Fullness of our ideas and maps and theories about humanity (and the world) at large, but also in the very Fullness of our own being. That the Integral AQAL Framework applies to “humanity (and the world)” means it also applies directly to you. These are all areas, we have seen, that apply not only to the world out there and the humans and other beings in it, but to the very dimensions of your own existence, your own being-in-the-world. These are areas that, you likely discovered, were already operating in you but you really didn’t know or suspect it—all of them being something like the grammar rules that we all follow without even thinking about it. But these rules have been discovered (by various knowledge communities, some of them going back thousands and thousands of years, others a mere decade or two old)—but they are there, they are real, they are impacting us daily, and again we have essentially one choice in the face of them all: will they exist and impact us in a conscious or an unconscious way? Will we be aware of them, or will we continue to let them completely blindside us? We do not have the choice to allow them or not allow them to exist, or to be impacted by them or not be impacted by them—they are already there, doing their job, and slamming into us minute by minute as they do so—and we can be aware of that, or we can remain ignorant and unaware of that. That is our choice—and our only choice, alas.

And—another “alas”—this means that a little bit of work—not a ton, but a bit—is definitely required. It takes a little bit of time and work to learn about these various areas, because, again, they’re not simply given. But we need to realize that deciding not to make this effort does not mean that these factors therefore cease to smack into us or affect us or alter us or whack us about in hundreds of ways. It means only that we have decided to suffer them in ignorance. We are actively choosing to let them impact us in a hidden fashion when we don’t do the little bit of work required to make them un-hidden. So this is totally unlike, say, learning to play the piano, where if you decide not to, then you will simply lack that skill. But deciding not to learn about these areas does not mean you will simply lack these areas; no, you will still have them, and they will continue operating on you and through you, but you just

won’t have any idea about what is really happening to you. In some ways this is a rotten deal; but in others, it’s hard to realize how lucky we are to be alive in an era where at least these 5 previously completely hidden areas of staggering importance are now all fully and freely available to our awareness. Astonishing, just astonishing!

For many individuals, particularly those at Integral levels, the entire prospect of a future learning about these areas of higher, greater potentials generates a deep, subtle, but profound Joy—a sense of coming home, of seeing who and what we really are, of discovering our one and only True Self and ultimate Suchness, and Showing Up in all possible worlds for this magic to take effect and radiantly shine through. It is a Happy tomorrow, for sure, starting with this timeless Now.

If this prospect is interesting to you, let me suggest starting with a few books of mine, such as The Integral Vision or perhaps the forthcoming work The Religion of Tomorrow, or Integral Spirituality. The co-authored Integral Life Practice is a guide to using the full AQAL Framework and all its dimensions to construct a truly integral transformative practice. It includes dozens of exercises, and for each one there is something called a “One-Minute Module,” which is an extremely effective condensed version of the practice designed to take no more than a minute to complete. These short practices work, very effectively! And there’s no believable way you can claim that you don’t have a minute a day to do a practice. People tell us this is one of the best parts of the whole book, so check it out! You might also explore IntegralLife.com or CoreIntegral.com or the Ken Wilber page on Facebook. Follow the links from there, or Google your way in.

You will find a large and worldwide network of individuals using an Integral Approach in over 60 different disciplines and areas, and places in your own locality where you can get involved. The field—as a fresh young field itself—is always looking for fresh young talent, so consider a future in any field—medicine, law, education, politics, art, therapy, coaching, consulting, anything really—and approach it through an Integral perspective (see “What to Do Next” later in this chapter). The future for such approaches is staggering. As more and more people continue to move into the actual Integral levels of development themselves, the demand for Integral everything will continue to grow explosively. So consider riding that ever-widening wave.

And then there is, of course, your own personal growth and development.

What we’ve discovered with all the areas we’ve looked at is that, since virtually everything in existence is the product of evolution (or Spirit-in-action), everything has a developmental trajectory. Not only can it be expanded and developed through horizontal skill acquisition—or aptitude; it can be expanded and developed through vertical growth and unfolding—or altitude. This is true for any elements in quadrants, levels, lines, states, and types. “Integral Mindfulness,” for example, can be practiced on any of those elements, because it engages a fundamental, central core of all evolutionary processes—namely, the subject of one moment becomes the object of the subject of the next moment. Practicing Integral Mindfulness on levels accelerates the growth and development through those levels; practicing it on lines accelerates the growth and development through those various lines; practicing it on quadrants accelerates the growth and development through any of those quadrants; and so on. Little subjects become objects until there is only Absolute Subjectivity, which can never be made an object (empty Consciousness without an object). And that Seer finally collapses into everything seen, resurrecting a prior unity consciousness or headless One Taste, a radical unity with all things and events in the entire Kosmos—your one and only true Condition.

When you are resting in that fundamental One Taste, Primordial Avoidance is nowhere to be found. Awareness arises naturally, effortlessly, spontaneously; and directly and evenly reflects (or is one with) every single thing and event in the Total Painting of All That Is. You can Witness that Total Painting by taking the stance of the Observing Self:

I have sensations, but am not those sensations. I have feelings, but am not those feelings. I have thoughts, but am not those thoughts. I am radically Free of all of those, and rest as the unmoving, unlimited, unrestricted pure Witness of all that arises, my original I AMness.

And go one step further and move “beyond the fourth,” beyond the Witness, into nondual unity. Let the entire sense of the Looker dissolve, by focusing on any object and allowing that object to exist all on its own,

pushing out the feeling of a Looker and just arising in the Field of Awareness all by itself, on its own, as a self-existing, self-arising, self-manifesting, self-aware entity. As you do so, the entire world “out there” will seem to be arising on this side of your face, right where your head used to be, and there will be no separation whatsoever between what is seeing and what is seen— the entire universe will be arising within your own open Field of Awareness, will be arising within you, and you are of One Taste with all of it—you can taste the sky, swallow the Pacific Ocean in a single gulp, as the sky turns into a big blue pancake and falls on your head—or where your head used to be. You no longer see the mountain, you are the mountain; you no longer feel the Earth, you are the Earth; you no longer watch the clouds, you are the clouds —in this ever-present, pure, non-dual unity consciousness. Witness the Total Painting or, further, become one with it. . . .

But notice: that Total Painting can be seen through any number of levels; it can be seen by any number of lines; it can be seen via any of 4 fundamental quadrants; it can be seen in any number of states; and by any number of types. The Integral One Taste includes an Awareness of all of those, each and every one of them brought into the sunlight of pure Awareness, and not allowed to hide out in their hidden corner of Kosmos, smashing into us unawares, denting and damaging our being without our even suspecting the cause. Allowing Primordial Avoidance to uncoil in the vast expanse of All Space, Awareness settles on each of those elements (quadrants, levels, lines, states, types) as they arise, and easily allows all of them perfect and open access to the Mirror Mind of All That Is. The separate-self sense—the self-contraction—has disappeared into the pure Witness, which itself has evaporated into the Total Painting of All That Is, such that there is no longer an “in here” versus an “out there,” but the Looker itself has vanished into everything looked at—the sensation of being a separate Looker has been replaced by the single sensation of the Total Painting of All That Is, with the “out there” becoming simply the What Is Arising, no within and no without, just the Arising. All fear has evaporated, with the disappearance of all separate “others” or “objects out there” into a unitary One Taste. All desire— as an actual gnawing lack—has disappeared as well, there being nothing outside of Awareness for it to want or crave. Everything is arising as it wishes, with no friction or tension or contraction anywhere—and no looking away, no turning away, no moving away—just the direct, effortless, full reflection of (or oneness with) every single thing and event in the Total

Painting of All That Is, arising just as it wishes to, self-existing and self-liberating into this Field of only One Taste.

A pain arises and is allowed to arise; a thought arises and is allowed to arise; an egoic craving arises and is allowed to arise; a horrid exterior view arises and is allowed to arise (even as my finite self is taking measures to remedy it, those measures are themselves just part of the Total Painting of All That Is). A profound relaxation at the core of being has dissolved the tension between subject and object into a unified Field of nondual Awareness, where the actual feeling of being a “subject in here” and the feeling of there being “objects out there” turn out to be one and the same feeling—when I feel one, then feel the other, they are the same feeling, that of Divine One Taste, timeless Now to timeless Now to timeless Now. The “Emptiness” of the space where my head seems to be arising (when I realize that “my head,” as I directly experience it, is not a thing but just an opening or clearing in which objects appear), and the world of Form “out there”—which I used to see as separate from me—turn out to be one and the same thing, as the “out there” world of Form is seen to be arising in this Empty space where my head used to be: Emptiness/Open-Space/headless and the world of Form-out-there are nondual, not-two, one and the same thing, so that the entire world is not separate from me, but is actually arising within me—and I am it.

And with that, it all has ended; with that, it all is truly over; with that, there is nothing but this, just this, in all the numberless worlds in all the endless times.

And this One Taste arises equally in all the realms and dimensions covered by the AQAL Framework. Just to highlight, as an example, the quadrants: as the “I” moves into the “I-I,” and that evaporates into nondual Suchness (the headless unity state), all 4 quadrants continue to arise (as the “diversity” aspect of the nondual “unity-in-diversity”). One’s own self is realized as one’s Unique Self (which is not itself apart from nondual Suchness). An individual is radically One with the single Spirit and Self of the entire Kosmos, but that Spirit, that Self, that Suchness, is looking out of that particular individual’s eyes, with a perspective and angle on the universe that is absolutely and inviolably unique to that person—the same ultimate Self as all sentient beings, yet through a different perspective. The result: each sentient being has a radically Unique Self (and Suchness), and finding this deeply, deeply universal and Kosmic Spirit that is simultaneously deeply, deeply Unique and Special, is to find one’s truest and deepest Supreme

Identity. You will come to see that the reason you came into being at all— among trillions and trillions of other sentient beings—is so that you could embody and express this one, single, unique perspective of Spirit, unique in the entire Kosmos—that is what you, as you, bring to God. That is how you, as you, complete God. You is what Spirit is doing in order to manifest a universe at all. Spirit and human complete and fulfill each other in this great Realization of that particular Supreme Identity. You come to understand, beyond the slightest doubt, why you are absolutely One with every sentient being in the entire Kosmos, and also absolutely unique in that version of Oneness—and that uniqueness is why you are here—to realize, embody, express, and communicate that unique perspective of Spirit on the Kosmos itself: there is only one of those among the zillions and zillions of other holons in all the world, and that is you.

Likewise, that core I-ness (the I-ness as universal Suchness uniquely perceived) is a being that includes, in its own inherent makeup, a thou-dimension (the Lower Left quadrant). Your ultimate and Absolute Subjectivity exists in a field of Absolute Intersubjectivity. All other thou’s that are “out there” also possess, in their own inherent makeup, a Unique Self which is a Unique perspective of, on, and by one Spirit, and that is their core just as it is yours. And while that One Spirit (or Emptiness) is radically the same in both of you, your perspectives (in Form) are inherently different and unique—and thus have something inherently important to offer each other. Thus, when you meet a you, a thou, in a truly authentic and genuine fashion, it is the I-Spirit in you resonating with the I-Spirit in them, and that resonance, approached humbly, courageously, openly, and compassionately, simply provides your own Total Self space (Total Self = antecedent Self/“I-I,” proximate self/“I,” and distal self/“me,” all set in Suchness) with a greater relative-realm, manifest-realm, integrative experience. Your deepest core Unique perspective is coming together with their deepest core Unique perspective, and those naturally lead toward a higher shared unity of both of their own specific perspectives, so that each of you grows your relative synthetic capacity, the worldviews of both of you expand more and embrace more and include more—as your “uniquenesses” are shared, expanding both. And this thou-component is not just, by any means, an “other” that is “out there,” with your two Selves coming together to find a higher unity—that thou-dimension is an intrinsic dimension of your very own being itself (it is your own Lower Left quadrant). The two thou’s can come together in

dialogical communication (or mutual understanding or mutual resonance) precisely because there is ultimately but one Self, one Spirit, at the core of each; but that One Spirit has manifest itself in a gazillion individual holons, each an opening or clearing of that One Spirit, yet through its own unique perspective and angle. So this mutual understanding is a real unity-in-diversity, and both the “unity” (One Spirit) and the “diversity” (in a gazillion number of unique perspectives) are very real, equally to be honored.
