In this sense, two separate sentient beings coming together and mutually exchanging anything is Spirit meeting Spirit, God talking to God, Goddess embracing Goddess, in an exuberant Love, Joy, Enfolding, Embrace. Each sentient being’s Total Self grows in the process. This dialogical—or more broadly and accurately, this dialectical (since the mutual exchange can occur on any and all levels, not just mental symbolic and communicative and “logical”)— this dialectical process is the core of your relationship and interaction with all other beings, particularly as you realize your own Deepest Condition and therefore start to see it everywhere in others as well. And this “thou-dimension of my own I” is indeed a component of your own being. We really do need each other to fully complete ourselves, because the other’s Unique perspective is just that—Unique to them, something that I can access only through sincerest dialectical inquiry with another Thou, and thus something that I cannot do merely and solely on my own—I need you, and you need me, to complete this radical circle (and the Lower Left quadrant—the “miracle of the We”—is where this completion occurs, which is why, indeed, this quadrant is an inherent component of my own deepest being-in-the-world). And this quadrant doesn’t become inherent in me after I have an interaction with an “other,” which is then superficially added to my being; it is there prior to any specific interaction with others, which simply provide the details for this intrinsic dimension.

In short, I am the Buddha we are. And you can say the same as well. Such for the Lower Left. This is all intrinsically expressed, manifested, and

put into action through my Upper Right quadrant—my exterior, objective, “physical” being and behavior, where “physical” or “material” is no longer just the lowest rung on the Great Chain of Being, but also the exterior dimension of every rung in the Great Chain. If I think a purely logical thought (orange Upper Left), nonetheless it has a correlate in brain waves in the triune brain (of the Upper Right). Even an experience of God-consciousness (in the Upper Left) has a correlative brain state (in the Upper

Right). Those material complexiflcations are not at a lower level than the interior consciousness states, but—as you can see on any 4-quadrant diagram —are their exteriors, at the same level. And “metaphysical” or “supernatural” realities are not “above” nature or beyond nature, but within nature, interior to nature (as the conscious “mind” is interior to the material “brain”—the mind cannot be reduced to the brain, but is not separate from it, either; all 4 quadrants are nonreducible realities).

And thus my very existence, on all levels, is inherently embodied in a physical/material form or exterior, which is the space in which I directly meet, see, and touch other sentient beings and their interiors as expressed in their exterior embodied actions. This physical space is where all metaphysical realities meet, see each other, smile at each other, touch each other, embrace each other—because here, in this physical Clearing, they bring the concrete dimensions of their own being into visible realities, ready and happy to be seen by others, joining in the dance of celebratory incarnation as the Spirit-made-flesh dances with each and every other manifestation of Spirit-made-flesh in a joyous tango of “happy to see and be seen.” My heart, my mind, my soul, my spirit, will find happy expression in my actual behavior, my specific actions, my authentic movements. My consciousness is deeply incarnated in my flesh; my interiors are inseparable from my exteriors. As these both become illuminated by my Unique Self and nondual Suchness, they both light up with a dance of recognition and join hands in the fullest manifestation of my own Unique view of and as Spirit.

And this goes all the way up, too, since “exteriors” include not only gross/physical energies—expressed in the typical material state—but also subtle and causal and nondual bodies/energies; subtle—expressed in everything from dreams to various meditative states to the bardo itself (the “in-between” state of rebirth); and causal bodies/energies of the dreamless, formless, infinite Abyss. The Trikaya doctrine of Mahayana Buddhism literally means the “Three Bodies” of Buddha—the Nirmanakaya (or gross Form body); the Sambhogakaya (or subtle-realm body); and the Dharmakaya (the pure causal/Emptiness body)—to which is often added the Svabhavikakaya—the nondual integration of all of them. The point is, these are bodies—actual concrete exteriors (which become subtler and subtler, gross to subtle to causal), and not just minds—their correlative interiors or consciousness; that is, the “body/energy” and “mind/consciousness” dimensions are, respectively, the Right Hand and Left Hand dimensions of

the same occasion, each arising together, existing together, and evolving together. The point is, every mind (or Upper Left) has a body (or Upper Right). And a Full Realization brings both of those online, together and inseparably interwoven. Talk of a “new consciousness” without talk of “a new body” is indeed disembodied mumbo jumbo.

And thus, with each action I take, all of me steps into each and every step. And I am Fully Present to the Present as I Fully Embody every interior in its corresponding exterior. This is not something I have to try to do; after Realization, it is something that occurs as naturally as thunder accompanying lightning.

But it definitely needs to be taken into account. And when, therefore, my “I” and your “thou” come together, we are meeting each other in fully embodied ways in a physical space. It is not one head talking to another head; it is one bodymind dancing fully with another bodymind. The fleshiness of all of these encounters cannot be forgotten. And nor is this merely a flesh encounter; it is a flesh-incarnating-mind/Spirit that is most real and most engaged—the reality of the Present is a 4-quadrant happening. And as I step into all 4 quadrants as fully illuminated by infinite Spirit and One Self, I am inhabiting both my universally infinite One Spirit—identical in all sentient beings (Schrödinger again: “Consciousness is a singular of which the plural is unknown”) and therefore the Oneness of my One Taste—but also as seen and reflected through this only, radically unique, superpersonalized complex of elements in all 4 quadrants, a complex the likes of which has existed nowhere in the universe, at no time in history, in any fashion like the one reflecting through me (and you as well, and every individual sentient being)—thus supplying the genuine “Manyness” or the real “diversity” in the “unity-in-diversity” or the “One-in-the-Many.” The One provides my Freedom; the Many provides my Fullness. With both dancing hand in hand, the deepest Joy imaginable rises from my Heart and floats above my Head, mixing with the heavenly Light of the universe originating there, whence it moves down into and through my body, focusing in my belly, the source of earthly Life in all manifest dimensions, Joy resonating with Joy, Radiance with Radiance, Freedom with Freedom, and Fullness with Fullness.

This one-Self/one-Spirit of mine is actually reflected in every “I” as God’s deepest Self-realization, in every “we” as God’s sincerest communication and worship, and in every “it” as God’s most gracious manifestation and concrete embodiment. Every “I,” God; every “we,” God’s sincerest worship; every

“it,” God’s most gracious form. And seeing such, I am Happy.

What to Do Next

The final—and in some ways the most common—question I am often asked about this Integral approach is how to actually enact it in real life:

I have a pretty good understanding of the Integral view, but I’m frustrated about ways to get it out there in the real world. I just don’t see how to use it to make an impact. What do you recommend?

First we have to understand the “good news, bad news” situation that is actually involved in being at an Integral level of development at this time in history. Remember, this Integral level is the very leading edge of evolution itself. As we’ve estimated, right now less than 5% of the world’s population is at 2nd-tier Integral—and that means 95% are at 1st-tier levels. And that means that 95% of the world’s population is actively anti-Integral, is deeply dedicated to Integral approaches not working, not being accepted, not being advanced. Each 1st-tier level thinks its truth and values are the only real truth and values in existence, and yet this crazy Integral level thinks all other levels have some sort of profound and significant reason for their existence. But if you are Integral, this means that the vast majority of the world’s population is dedicated to seeing you fail.

Further, the vast number of those who are at the Integral level do not know that they are at the Integral level—they haven’t yet self-identified, they don’t know that they are coming from a real level of human development, that the thoughts and ideas that they are having are not crazy or weird or sick (as people around them keep telling them they are). They usually went through a period of trying to convince their friends and associates of how important some of these ideas seemed, but after having not much luck with that, they tended to give up, and return to flying at the altitude that most of their associates are flying at (usually orange or green). “When in 1st-tier Rome, fly 1st tier. . . .”

You likely were one of those. It will take a little more time for at least 10% of the population to reach 2nd tier. Historically, when 10% of the population reached the leading-edge level (whether that was amber, or orange, or green), there was a “tipping point” that allowed the values of the leading edge to gain

a certain prominence and acceptance throughout culture. (Thus, when 10% of the population reached what was leading-edge orange at the time, we had the French and American revolutions, the rise of representative democracy, the Constitution of the United States, the legal end of slavery in every rational-industrial nation on the entire planet—something that had never happened before in all of history; and so on—even though only 10% of population actually believed those ideas, they were somehow deeply acceptable to them. Likewise, when 10% hit green, we had the revolutions of the ’60s, with the civil rights movement, the worldwide environmental movement, the rise of personal and professional feminism, hate-crime legislation, and so on.)

When 10% of the population hits Integral, we can expect the most profound tipping point in the history of humanity, for the simple reason that, for the first time ever in history, a truly inclusive and embracing level of development will have an influence on the culture at large (in contrast to all of the previous transformations, where the leading-edge levels, all being 1st

tier, were each exclusive, marginalizing, and ultimately oppressive in one way or another). We have no idea what a truly inclusive, non-marginalizing society would look like—we’ve never had one before. But it will be radically different from anything we’ve seen—a momentous leap of meaning, but now in a culture-wide reach.

In the meantime, we are stuck with our basic “good news, bad news” situation: leading edge, but not yet tipping-point influential. Because Integral isn’t well known or widely recognized, there are as yet few economic markets or jobs for individuals at Integral levels; they generally have to work a normal job, and either try to slowly work Integral ideas into their professional situations, or keep them quiet and in the closet. It is, indeed, lonely at the top.

So especially for that interim period, there are several recommendations. First is to continue to learn and study Integral Theory. The more you can develop a fairly accurate map of the territory that you now inhabit, the better the situation will be. As you continue to think differently, you will continue to act differently. New behaviors will start to announce themselves; next moves will increasingly become more obvious; you will see more and more openings for this new understanding in all the various areas of your life.

This means searching the Web for Integral groups, online courses, books, videos, discussion groups, salons, graduate and post-graduate courses. Start to build a community, even if it is online and largely through the Net, where

you can find others who share your altitude of development and some of the major maps of that altitude. There are already a fair number of these out there, and you might be surprised at the wide variety of views that have been sparked by this general Integral approach.

Locally, at home, begin again trying to find at least a few friends or colleagues with whom you can share your Integral understanding. At your job there might be opportunities where taking a more Integral approach will have a definite impact on a particular task or work assignment, and if you present an Integral overview of the situation—simple, clear, concise, with a minimum of jargon or technical terms—you might be surprised at how open your colleagues are (particularly if you language the proposal in the terms of the major level they are at—usually orange or green).

Likewise, with friends, again begin approaching select individuals who you think might be at, or close to, an Integral level of development. Perhaps present them with a good introductory book on Integral, confide how much it has meant to you, and ask them to please give it an open-minded look. Even one friend in a town or city who shares your interest in Integral can make a huge difference.

If you succeed in finding a friend or two who are equally excited by an Integral approach, consider forming a local salon or Integral group. Put up flyers, take out ads in local newspapers, create a presence on the Web, find a location for meetings, and open up a meeting each month to the public. Discussing items from current local and global news is often a good way to introduce the benefits of a more Integral view. Some Integral proponents have started a weekly or monthly Web dialogue, open to any who would like to participate. Participants generally get excited and end up spreading the word to their friends and colleagues. If you start a local Integral salon, consider forming relationships with salons in other cities (found via a Web search—there are several).

If your ambitions run wider, consider actually looking at ways to make your profession an Integral profession. Much of this is still virgin territory, and your own creative gifts and talents will find a happy outlet, especially if you succeed. But there has also been a fair amount of work done in many areas—in the professional journal of the field, The Journal of Integral Theory and Practice, over 60 different disciplines have been fully reinterpreted using an AQAL Integral Framework (and other Integral approaches), and all of them ended up much more complete and satisfying and functionally effective.

In some cases, with a group of professionals—medicine, for example—one colleague starts out practicing an Integral Medicine (simply created from that doctor’s understanding of AQAL from his or her own reading, and sometimes supplemented by finding other doctors, online via search, who are already doing the same, and getting guidelines from them as well), and invariably (in every case that I am aware of), this doctor’s patient load begins to increase, their costs go down, and pretty soon the rest of their colleagues want to know what’s going on—and in many of those cases, the entire medical group switched to Integral Medicine, and are all genuinely happy with the outcome.

Although your Integral altitude of development was given to you for reasons that nobody fully understands, to actually apply it and bring it fully into the world requires skillful means and occasionally some real work. But you are following the deepest insights of heart, mind, and soul when you do so—the very depth in you translates into the world, and you start to have an impact on the society around you that does indeed reflect that “momentous leap of meaning” that has already descended on you. Whatever happens in the “I” space, the Upper Left quadrant, tends to spread—via its actual behavior in the Upper Right—to create cultural “we” spaces (Lower Left) as well as social “its” institutions (Lower Right) that reflect the same degree of altitude. Your awareness can’t help but spill out and into the world around you, leaving an indelible impact.

As you start to increase your success in a particular field, consider taking that to its own next stages. You can certainly consider writing a book, detailing your actions and giving suggestions for those who would like to follow. You can create a website, either yourself or by joining with work colleagues or interested friends. This will attract similarly interested professionals and lay individuals from around the country and around the world, and you might look to then create associations or guilds or collectives of those with similar interests and professional fields. From there it’s a short step to annual conferences, and from there, a possible journal. Likewise online courses, both for your clients and for other professionals who want to create an Integral approach in their own field. By that time, you will have almost certainly discovered several groups who are also practicing their versions of your Integral profession, and you might find it worthwhile to form partnerships with any or all of them.

In your own life, you will likely (though not necessarily) have taken up a

path of Waking Up as you pursue the higher-state potentials of your own Integral possibilities. You can rest assured that virtually none of the paths of Waking Up that you find will be aware of the stages of Growing Up; and if you feel so moved, you might start, very gingerly and carefully, by speaking to people in the spiritual organization about these areas (such as stages of spiritually Growing Up). You should realize that some of these groups will be headed by people who are highly advanced in state development (of Waking Up), and not all that advanced in stages of Growing Up. If you happen to find a teacher who is at 2nd tier in Growing Up, they will almost certainly be interested in aspects of the Integral approach, including structure-stages of Growing Up. If they are at green (which the majority appear to be, based on their teachings and writings), they almost certainly will not be interested. Orange can go either way, but has no inherent bias against it, so it could prove positive, depending on the teacher. But you might have to resign yourself to simply listening to what this teaching has to say about states, and pretty much ignore everything else.

There are various ways to introduce stages of Growing Up to a group of Waking Up practitioners. One way is to select any core set of values and aspirations of the particular system (or the group) and explain to the group how those values and aspirations actually develop along not just one but two scales. One developmental scale is through the various state-stages of Waking Up as they are presented in this particular spiritual system; the group will be almost certainly aware of a version of this, even if it’s a very reduced form. However, teachers and practitioners at the green level in the United States have virtually erased most stages entirely from the picture because, having equated growth hierarchies with dominator hierarchies, they believe that all “ranking” schemes are oppressive and marginalizing. On the contrary, growth hierarchies are just the opposite, with every higher level more inclusive and less oppressive and less dominating; but this is a point that the green view almost inherently does not recognize, and thus they usually maintain that all you need to do is simply meditate: you’re already Enlightened, so simply sit to express your already-Enlightened nature, and don’t worry about any so-called stages. This view acknowledges only absolute truth and ignores relative truth, whereas in the ultimate nondual realization both are fully included—something this approach fails to do pretty thoroughly. So you’ll have to look out for that in any attempt to introduce the Integral view.

But however you introduce it, the idea is that, in addition to whatever meditative development one can undergo, there is a second type of development, and that is the type that modern Western psychology has discovered: namely, the structure-stages of Growing Up. Explain that these stages of unfolding are like the grammar rules of a language, and they can’t be seen by simply introspecting, which is why no meditation system anywhere in the world is aware of them. It might help to give a simple example of this, such as Carol Gilligan’s 4 stages of female moral growth: selfish, or egocentric; care, or ethnocentric; universal care, or worldcentric; and integrated, or Kosmocentric. For Gilligan, this stage means integrating masculine and feminine modes; in our version, it also means treating not only all humans but all sentient beings fairly, including Gaia, the global environment, and the entire manifest realm; this is in keeping with the continued developmental expansion of identity from “me” to “us” to “all of us” to “all of reality.”

Then, using the particular set of values or aspirations you’ve chosen as examples—in this case, we’ll assume the group you are with is Buddhist, and so we’ll use the traditional group of 6 paramitas of Mahayana Buddhism (10 in Vajrayana). The Sanskrit word paramita has two meanings: in one sense, as a virtuous quality, it is often translated “perfection” in English; in the other sense, it means “that which has gone beyond the other shore.” We could therefore define “paramita” as a quality helpful for passing to the “other shore” of Enlightenment—that is, a multiple intelligence useful in gaining Enlightenment. These paramitas are indeed very like multiple intelligences and can, I think, be directly counted as actual intelligences in most cases. They are skillful, intelligently driven disciplines and qualities that are said to be either the means of attaining Enlightenment or the actual expressions of an always-already Enlightened mind. The 6 paramitas are (1) generosity, or caring giving; (2) moral discipline; (3) patience, or acceptance; (4) will, or joyful diligence; (5) meditative concentration; and (6) nondual awareness. To these 6 the Vajrayana adds (7) skillful means, (8) aspiration, (9) strength, and (10) primordial wisdom, or primordial awareness.

Now, the point about those intelligences or qualities is that not only do they grow and develop through the state-stages of Waking Up—which is certainly important (i.e., there is a gross, subtle, causal, turiya, and turiyatita version of all of them)—but they also grow and develop through the structure-stages of Growing Up. Thus, each of the paramitas has a “dual

center of gravity,” that is, a version—not only in a given stage of Waking Up (gross, subtle, causal, etc.)—but also a version in a given stage of Growing Up (such as egocentric, ethnocentric, worldcentric, and integral forms—or any rainbow altitude level); and those are relatively independent developments, you can be low, medium, and high in any of those sequences.

And the stages of Growing Up deserve extra attention, because, although any specific path of Waking Up is almost certainly aware of the state-stages that their practice goes through, they are almost certainly not aware of the structure-stages that their practice is also negotiating (and in an unconscious and unaware and haphazard fashion). Particularly if one has used the Gilligan model as an introductory example of the stages of Growing Up, it’s easy to run through each of the paramitas and demonstrate how important it is to be well developed in this scale as well (and this scale can be kept simple at first, using, e.g., “low, medium, high, and full” or “egocentric, ethnocentric, worldcentric, and integrated,” in keeping with Gilligan’s simplified scheme). A practitioner doesn’t want, for example, generosity extended only to oneself, or even to a special group of people, or even all people, but to all sentient beings (Kosmocentric and integrated). And we’ve already seen that “nondual unity” awareness is indeed a unity, but only with the highest world that has actually and developmentally unfolded. Thus, at ethnocentric, it is a unity only with the world of one’s chosen path and the people on it; and many Buddhist teachers remain at this level of structure development. There is currently—to give a very negative example, but one that shows all the more the need for including the Growing Up scale—a very militant Buddhist movement in Southeast Asia, whose leader refers to himself as “the Burmese Bin Laden” and teaches that all Muslims should be literally murdered; he has himself overseen attacks where numerous Muslims have been killed (he says this doesn’t violate Buddhist precepts because it is not an active attack but only an attack in self-defense, since all Muslims want all Buddhists dead). But Muslims aren’t even humans, he teaches, but are of the “animal realm,” and thus cannot attain Enlightenment. He is reputed to be of significant meditative accomplishment—but even if one accepted that he is at genuine turiyatita or nondual unity consciousness, his structure-development altitude is clearly ethnocentric (mythic and absolutistic).

Likewise, Zen at War is a book of some of the most respected Zen masters around, and they are expounding purely ethnocentric recommendations of militarism, authoritarianism, murder or war, and other prejudiced notions.1

Their Enlightened awareness simply supports their ethnocentric prejudices, because their nondual Suchness looks through the hidden maps of their structure development, and if it is at amber ethnocentric, then that is what the world actually looks like—that is, the Enlightened world is thought to look exactly like that amber ethnocentric version because that is the map through which the world unknowingly is viewed. And since none of the Traditions are aware of these hidden structure maps, none of them know how to spot and rectify these inadequacies. Hence, this is why we need to include these stages of Growing Up with our stages of Waking Up. Examples like this can often get the point across fairly strongly.

Figure 5.1. Vertical levels of development (Growing Up) in the paramitas—a psychograph of a particular individual showing their paramita growth. (To

view this figure in a larger format, visit www.shambhala.com/integralmeditation.)

Of course, we could also say that the structure-stages of Growing Up in the paramitas are archaic, magic, mythic, rational, pluralistic, and integral (or any vertical axis taken from a well-established developmental model), and that would be true. But the simple 4 stages we have been using, starting with Gilligan, will do just fine (“low/egocentric,” “medium/ethnocentric,” “high/worldcentric,” and “full/integrated”), and can be displayed as in figure 5.1.

If you succeed in showing a group the importance of including Growing Up with Waking Up, then you might explore further the possibilities of getting this group involved with a real Integral Spirituality, in this case, Integral Buddhism (for which you might consult my book The Fourth Turning). This would involve helping your group move through an

emergence of a yet more evolved Buddha Dharma. There are several teachers in each of the Great Traditions who have already made this type of move, and so far all seem deeply satisfied.

If you do so, you might want to contact, and work with, other Buddhist teachers and practitioners who have already begun working on an Integral Spirituality and together collaborate on creating a “Buddhist conveyor belt”—that is, the basic Buddhist teachings and practices put into the words, language, and framework of each of the major structure-levels of Growing Up (so that we have a magic version, a mythic version, a rational version, a pluralistic version, and an integral version of the Buddha Dharma); individuals at each of those levels will thus more easily and readily be able to understand and implement those teachings. Individuals are already at one or the other of those overall stages anyway, and they are already translating Buddhist teachings according to the limitations and restraints of their level (they have no choice),2 so having that already done accurately and authentically for them would be a tremendous help. This, of course, is something that needs to be done with every major Great Tradition, to help move them from a place of almost always being stuck at premodern, ethnocentric, mythic-literal stages, where they are a constant source of conflict, aggression, even war, to more worldcentric modern and postmodern stages, where they can fulfill their own highest potentials of increased love, compassion, peace, and harmony.

This Integral view of human growth and development (across quadrants, levels, lines, states, and types) is one of the first consistent and coherent world philosophies yet to emerge, and as such has had an impact on movements ranging from the “Indie Spiritualist” to the “New Monasticism” to “Interspirituality”—and, across the board, the entire range of professions and vocations, from education to business to politics to medicine to art, and so on. At the very least, we note that a radically novel, unprecedented level of consciousness and culture is headed our way with unstoppable evolutionary force. Known variously as “integrated,” “systemic,” “systems of systems,” “strategic,” “wholistic,” “inclusive,” “comprehensive,” “cross-paradigmatic,” “integral,” “2nd tier,” it is, by every single account, that momentous leap of meaning. As an actual level or structure or wave of development, this new level of consciousness will eventually underlie every single human activity in existence—at work, in relationships, in faith, in business, in parenting, in play—simply because every single human activity, of those in whom this

radical level emerges, will be driven and underwritten and authored by this new and integrated level. It’s not a matter of choosing to be moved by this Integral level or not—those humans who continue their growth and development will run into this as a normal, natural, universal stage of human growth. They will have no choice, if they grow, but to grow into and through this level. And this Integral level will produce Integral approaches, Integral models, Integral maps, Integral practices, in each and every human activity there is, simply because it will be the new “hidden map” that is driving virtually all human thought and action at that level.

But, in addition to growing into this level, we have a chance—that is, students of an Integral have a chance—to make a fair amount of this “hidden map” un-hidden; we can be aware of its general patterns, its fundamental principles, its basic contours, even if, when we first move into this stage, the fundamental patterns themselves will be part of the “embedded unconscious,” or those basic forms with which we are so identified as subject that we can’t see them as object. This is a normal, natural, desirable aspect of development, and something we wouldn’t want to avoid even if we could. Rather, the initial goal, when one is first at any level, is to identify with that level and learn how to interpret and translate the world according to that level’s new map, those new rules of grammar, which are more inclusive, more whole, more unified, more differentiated-and-integrated, and more embracing that any of our previous levels. Thus, it’s a new world, and it takes a fair amount of practice, trial-and-error learning, and re-interpreting previously understood aspects of reality, all of which have to be re-learned, re-known, re-interpreted.

And generally speaking, individuals are not aware of the new but still-hidden map that is driving their thought and behavior—they simply intuit it, and stumble forward with the map thoroughly hidden; but it is still acting to determine the overall direction and form of their every thought, feeling, and action. If they are aware of the characteristics of the new stage they are at, then that “conscious map” simply helps them adjust and accommodate to their new “hidden map” (which still remains hidden in direct form). But they do learn to translate from this new level with greater ease, clarity, and efficiency, simply because their “conscious map” tells them what their “hidden map” is attempting to do.

Given that the new, (r)evolutionary, 2nd-tier levels are indeed Integral, then nothing will help this conscious accommodation more, at this stage, than a consciously held Integral map or super-map, and that is something to which

the AQAL Framework (or any other integral map you might discover or create) will definitely contribute. And as the first truly inclusive or integral level in all of humankind’s history, it is the first awareness in all of history that is actually competent—truly competent—to house a genuine “unity consciousness” from the Waking Up dimension of human development. This means that all of the previous Enlightenment, Awakening, or Emancipation experiences throughout history were, by the standards of their times, indeed Enlightenment (“one with the highest structure and the highest state that have emerged at any given time in history and evolution”), but Spirit-in-action, Eros, evolution itself has continued to move forward, creating greater and greater Fullness of Form, until this, its most recent emergent—the first truly inclusive and embracing Fullness-of-Form level of any era in history—and one that is finally competent to house a genuine “unity consciousness.”

In fact, we are on the eve of the single greatest transformation of human consciousness ever to emerge, anywhere, anytime. That tsunami is now headed unmistakably in our direction, and is due to start washing up on our shores in the next decade or two, as integral levels hit 10% or so. Those who adopt the Integral Framework now are simply the ones that, when the tsunami hits, will already have the surfboard concessions, allowing them to help others ride this new and stunning wave.

And once you are at this new wave, you will intrinsically wish to see it moved into the exterior world “out there,” helping more and more people embrace their own truest, deepest, and highest potentials. Taking the Integral View into the World becomes a constant motivation for you, becomes how you walk your talk, live your purpose, fashion a value-driven life based on the deepest and widest and highest values available to you, an utterly satisfying and self-fulfilling life indeed, chocolate for the soul. And so you take your integral interior and start moving it into your exteriors, one step at a time, but always. Your very integrity depends upon your talk matching your walk—and your “talk” is how you talk to yourself when you’re all alone, not just how you talk to people when you might be acting “diplomatic” or less than fully revealing, but your deepest, truest core thoughts, about what really and truly does matter most. Once you see the Integral Worldspace, it’s just not something you can turn your back on; that toothpaste will definitely not go back in the tube—it’s bigger, higher, wider, deeper, and so are you, your world, your awareness, your identity. You have come home.

It’s at the point now where virtually every area and discipline and

profession in the United States (and the world) has at least a few people who are fully and explicitly practicing and promoting an Integral approach in that area (from business to medicine to education to politics to art to music to nursing to therapy to leadership to consulting to parenting to coaching to psychology, philosophy, and spirituality). Look for them. Many of them are on the Web. Contact them if you feel so moved. Generate friendships with such individuals, and look for ways to work with them in spreading the word. Offer to help them in any way you can, and accept any help they might have to offer you. Slowly but steadily grow your “Integral Rolodex,” and be on the lookout for ways that you can offer an Integral approach in any number of ways. Don’t worry if you find many somewhat different approaches to Integral, some of them occasionally belligerent and bellicose—humans will be humans, and all-too-human is still the case with Integral. Don’t let that throw you; listen to the different approaches, and stick with the one that makes the most sense to you. But overall, if Integral isn’t moving forward, it’s because you are not moving forward. And that holds for all of us.

If it all becomes too frustrating or too disappointing, always go back to the foundation—simply return to a simple but sincere study of the Integral approach. Take 15 to 20 minutes daily to read and reflect on this approach, from any number of sources. Let it continue to saturate your being. Remember that, in its deepest core, the Integral approach is not a theory or philosophy but an actual stage of worldwide human development; it’s not like learning, say, deconstruction, which is a particular theory that you may, or may not, believe in. Rather, it’s a universal structure-stage of actual human development—it’s a territory that is itself ultimately driving the various maps of itself, and that territory is very real, and it will always act to correct and adjust your ideas about it. It’s an actual altitude of human development—it’s like amber or orange or green; individuals at those levels cannot but think and act according to the broad parameters and restraints of those levels; and that Integral altitude will always act as your main teacher and guide, and in itself, it cannot be wrong—it can only be just what it is, and that is what will ultimately guide you: a structure that is the result of 14 billion years of evolution’s trial and error to produce the most whole, most unified, most differentiated, most universal and most unique holon ever in existence—you.

So continue to open yourself to Integral influence. Continue to think differently, and you will continue to behave differently. And your behavior will begin to transform the world, both in your authentic actions and in the

Form that you are laying down in that great storage bin of Form in the Kosmos. The very Form of your Awareness will be ready to have done with the old and broken world, and instead usher in a world that truly cares, a world that fully completes, a world that utterly embraces and includes, and doesn’t look back at a sorry history of so much less than that.

As you continue to look for ways to allow that inner understanding to spill out and into the world, what all this kind of activity is doing is allowing you to speak your authentic heart and mind and soul, expressing in your outward actions the deepest truths of your inner being, allowing those inner realizations to reverberate in the outer world, resonating with all 4 quadrants of your very own being-in-the-world, covering them with Joy, Grace, and Gratitude. The inherent drive of your inner awareness is to reverberate through every “I” and “we” and “it” and “its” in existence—deeply changing your “I,” and profoundly altering your behavior (“it”), and radically transforming your culture (“we”), and rejuvenating your social institutions (“its”)—vibrating with a Spirit that is Self-Realized in every “I,” communicated and shared in every “we,” and manifest and seen in every “it”—with every “I” being God’s most authentic Self, every “we” God’s sincerest worship, and every “it” God’s most gracious manifestation and radiant ornament. The inner thrill of your own realization and awareness crashes into the surrounding world, transmitting a similarly ecstatic Presence and self-fulfilling Genuineness—God to God to God, Spirit to Spirit to Spirit —so that in all the worlds, in all the times, in all the dimensions there are, there is only Spirit, wherever you look.

There is only Spirit. Late on a crystal fall evening, early snow sparkling under a freshly new

moon—round and white and full in the sky—millions of humans asleep in their abodes, desires in their hearts, yearnings in their souls, passing through unknown state after unknown state, wondering what it all is about, dreaming mostly, mostly dreaming. And you are pulled back into it all, pulled by a timeless promise somewhere in your heart to awaken all souls to their One and Only; hauntingly ringing out, announcing itself, amid the millions of snowflakes, micro-miniature rainbows of moonlight, all shimmering in a purple haze of moon on snow everlastingly, the razor haze piercing your skin with its intensity, penetrating all the way to bone, the whole scene one of intermediating interwovenness, dancing in the moonlit glow, tunelessly alive and radiantly, luminously all-encompassing. I suddenly blink my eyes, once

but hard, and it all entirely disappears.

I appreciate enormously your accompanying me on this extraordinary venture, and I’m sending you all of my very best wishes. . . . Aloha, or till we meet again. . . .

Brian Daizen Victoria, Zen at War, 2nd ed. (Lanham, MD: Rowman & Littlefield, 2006). 2. See The Fourth Turning for actual examples of Buddhist schools at each of these major levels.
